# NyxOS — Agent Context

## Goal
Evolve NyxOS into a functional x86_64 kernel with filesystem, networking, shell, process multitasking, GUI, multimedia, and audio.

## Commit rule
Every commit must keep these in sync: GitHub Wiki (`nyx-os.wiki.git`), website (`docs/`), README.md, and any version/status references in AGENTS.md. Always check what changed and update accordingly before committing.

## Build & Test
- Cross-compiler: host GCC with `-m64` (or `x86_64-elf-gcc` for cross)
- Build (PowerShell): `.\build.ps1` → `kernel/nyx-kernel.bin`
- Build (make): `make -C kernel` → `kernel/nyx-kernel.bin`
- Run (GUI): `.\run.ps1`
- Run (serial debug): `.\run.ps1 -Mode serial`
- Run (with net): `.\run.ps1 -Mode net`
- Run (with disk): append `-hda ext2-test.img` to QEMU args
- ISO: `tools/build.sh` → `NyxOS.iso`
- Repo: `https://github.com/kazah-png/nyx-os`
- GitHub Wiki: live at `https://github.com/kazah-png/nyx-os/wiki` — 16 pages pushed; `git clone https://github.com/kazah-png/nyx-os.wiki.git`

| v1.0.0 | Base kernel |

| v3.1.0 | RTC driver, more syscalls (open/read/close/getpid/sbrk/fsize/exec), libc (printf/malloc/snprintf), initramfs auto-boot, desktop polish (right-click, settings, wallpaper) |
| v4.0.0 | Full x86_64 native (long mode, 4-level paging, syscall/sysret, ELF64, ring 3), clean 32-bit dead code removal, docs corrected |
| v4.1.0 | Higher-half kernel mapping (PML4[256] mirror), user/kernel page-table isolation (user has no identity mapping), CR3 switching in ISR/IRQ/syscall entries, scheduler uses next_cr3 |
| v4.2.0 | NX bit (No-Execute) + SMEP (Supervisor Mode Execution Prevention), PAGE_NX added to user stack/heap/data, Local APIC + I/O APIC init, CPUID detection, IST (Interrupt Stack Table) for double fault (#8) and NMI (#2), repo cleanup (README x86_64, .gitignore, AGENTS.md) |
| v5.0.0 | Full GUI application suite: Text Editor (file open/save, cursor nav), Image Viewer (test pattern, zoom/pan), Sound Test (PC speaker + SB16 sine/square/sweep). Brighter wallpaper gradient (sky-blue + grass). 8 desktop icons. **All placeholders replaced with real apps.** No crashes, zero warnings in build. |
| v5.1.0 | Stability and bugfix release: TSS struct alignment fix (IST pointers shifted 4 bytes early → triple fault on double fault/NMI), GDT limit correction, APIC/PIC IRQ masking fix (PIC left fully masked when APIC active), switch_to_user_process inline asm operand reversal fix, VGA 8x16 font data corruption fix (marker bytes inserted in each glyph → garbage text in GUI). QEMU display changed to sdl for Windows compatibility. |
| v5.3.0 | Login system: boot animation (~5s, NyxOS-themed, 23-step progress bar), credential storage on EXT2 (/etc/passwd), framebuffer login screen with keyboard input, default user nyx/nyx, fallback when no EXT2 disk, login failure → reboot, success → launch desktop. |
| v5.4.0 | Audit/hardening pass: fixed clean-build break (`sha256.h` pulled host `<stdint.h>`, conflicting with kernel int types); build is warning-free again (0 warnings from ~160 — pointer-truncation casts routed through `uintptr_t`, keyboard keymap over-initializers trimmed, dead code removed). **Security:** ring-3 syscall boundary hardened — user pointers validated against the canonical user half, and userspace now gets small integer fds via a translation table instead of raw (forgeable) kernel VFS handles (closed an info-leak + arbitrary-kernel-r/w reachable via `exec`). Panic handler now prints faulting RIP/CS/ring/error. |
| v5.5.0 | **Ring-3 userspace now actually runs.** `exec <elf>` loads an ELF64, enters ring 3, services syscalls, and returns to the shell on exit. Fixed the full chain: (1) EFER.SCE was never enabled → `syscall` #UD'd; (2) LSTAR pointed at the low link address, unmapped under the user CR3 → set it to the PML4[511] higher-half alias; (3) `syscall_entry` clobbered RAX/RBX (and truncated user RSP) *before* SAVE_REGS — rewrote it to save all user GPRs first using RIP-relative ops (no scratch reg), with `kernel_rsp` stored as a higher-half alias; (4) added `copy_from_user`/`copy_to_user` that walk the user page tables to physical (masking bits 51:12 — `~0xFFF` alone kept the NX bit and produced non-canonical → #GP); (5) return via `iretq` (NASM's bare `sysret` is the 32-bit form and truncates RSP); (6) `switch_to_user_process` uses a small setjmp/longjmp (`ku_setjmp`/`ku_longjmp`) so exit() unwinds to the caller instead of halting. Verified: init.elf prints, getpid/malloc(sbrk)/snprintf/write/exit all work, control returns to boot. |
| v5.6.0 | **Userspace cleanup + heap bug.** (1) `exec` now reaps the process on exit: `free_page_directory` (paging.c) walks the user half (PML4[0..510], skips the 511 kernel mirror) freeing every leaf frame + table; `reap_user_process` (process.c) also frees the kernel/context stack (`kernel_stack-4096` is the kmalloc base — `proc->stack` is a *middle* pointer, so the old `destroy_process` `kfree(proc->stack)` corrupted the heap; fixed). Verified clean over repeated execs. (2) **Heap bug:** `kmalloc` sent every request ≤ `SLAB_MAX_OBJ` (1024) to the slab, but the slab caches only went up to 512, so `slab_alloc` returned NULL for 505..1016 B and kmalloc returned NULL *instead of falling back to the heap* — every mid-size allocation failed (e.g. VFS file writes: `/home/user/welcome.txt` was silently empty). Fixed: kmalloc falls back to heap on slab miss; two header magics (`_SLAB`/`_HEAP`) so kfree routes correctly regardless of size; added the missing 1024 slab class. (3) `SYS_WRITE` now routes file fds (≥3) through the ufd table to `vfs_write`, so userspace can create/write files (not just stdout/stderr). init.elf exercises the full file-I/O path (open/create/write/read/close) as a regression test. |
| v5.7.0 | **Per-fd offsets / streaming file I/O.** Added `vfs_pread`/`vfs_pwrite` (offset-aware; pwrite grows + zero-fills without discarding existing content). The userspace fd table (`ufd_*` in syscall.c) now tracks a byte offset per open fd, advanced by each `SYS_READ`/`SYS_WRITE`, so ring-3 reads stream across calls and writes append instead of overwriting. Kernel-internal `vfs_read`/`vfs_write` (offset 0) are unchanged — no blast radius on the shell's one-shot open→read→close callers. Verified: init.elf writes "Hello, " + "userspace file I/O!" in two calls and streams it back in two reads. |
| v5.7.1 | **Fd-based I/O routes to EXT2 mounts (persistent files).** Previously only the path-based helpers (`vfs_write_file`/`vfs_cat_file`) reached a mounted FS; the fd-based path (`vfs_open`/`read`/`write`/`close` — used by `echo >`, head/tail/grep/sort/wc/diff, the editor, and userspace) hit the ramdisk and never persisted, and `/mnt` isn't even a ramdisk node. Now `vfs_open` detects a mount path (`vfs_find_mount`) and returns a transient **mount-backed node** preloaded from the FS (`read_file`); `vfs_write`/`vfs_pwrite` flush the whole node back via `write_file`; `vfs_close` frees it. Added a node free-list so these per-open nodes don't exhaust the 128-node pool. Verified across reboot: a file written to `/mnt` via `vfs_open`+`vfs_write` is read back on the next boot with the same disk image. (Ext2 write engine + persistence were already solid; this wires the fd layer to it.) |
| v5.7.2 | **Fd-based `readdir` on EXT2 mounts (file-manager can browse disk).** `vfs_open` on a mount path now probes `readdir` (ext2_readdir returns −1 for non-dirs) — if it's a directory it loads all entries once into the mount-backed node; `vfs_readdir` serves them by index and `vfs_close` frees them. Completes the mount FS surface: fd-based file read/write **and** directory listing on `/mnt` (path-based `ls`/`mkdir`/`rm` already worked and persist). Verified: `vfs_open("/mnt")` + `vfs_readdir` loop lists `lost+found/`, files, and created subdirs with correct dir/file typing. |
| v5.7.3 | **Networking works — DHCP completes** (`dhcp` command gets a lease from QEMU slirp). The stack was 100% broken; fixed 7 bugs, chief among them a byte-order bug that killed *every* packet: (1) **IP header checksum stored little-endian** — `ip->checksum = ip_checksum()` wrote the network-order value LE-reversed, so slirp silently dropped all our IP frames (found via `-object filter-dump` pcap: checksum verified to 0x649b not 0xffff). (2) **`ip_send` ARP-resolved broadcast** (255.255.255.255) → hung; now uses the broadcast MAC directly. (3) **RTL8139 RX ring** re-derived the read offset from `CAPR+16` but wrote it back without `−16` (asymmetric, misaligned after packet 1) and never checked BUFE — rewrote to track the offset itself, check the empty bit, and init CAPR=0xFFF0. (4) **RCR** missing MXDMA/RXFTH fields. (5) **DHCP magic cookie** stored byte-reversed and (6) at BOOTP offset 240 instead of 236. (7) **DHCP poll loop** had no inter-poll delay (finished before replies arrived; timer is dead so it busy-waits on port 0x80). Verified end-to-end: DISCOVER→OFFER→REQUEST→ACK, lease 10.0.2.15. **Still TODO** (same byte-order class of bug, now unmasked): DNS (`hdr->flags`/return IP host-order), TCP/HTTP (seq/ack/ports/checksum), and the `ifconfig` IP *display* order (shows 10.0.2.15 reversed). |
| v5.7.4 | **Full network stack: DNS + TCP + HTTP work.** Building on v5.7.3's DHCP, fixed the rest of the host↔network byte-order bugs (all masked until the IP checksum was fixed): **DNS** — `qdcount`/`flags` stored LE (query had 256 questions → dropped), returned/parsed IPs and the timeout loop (used dead `tick_count` → infinite hang); **ARP** — sender/target IPs written high-byte-first (asked "who has 3.2.0.10"), `sleep()` hang, host-order static gateway entry with the wrong MAC (removed); **TCP** — `offset_flags`/`window`/checksum stored LE, checksum pseudo-header + RX `offset_flags` in the wrong order, and `payload_len` counted Ethernet padding (60-byte min frame added 2 phantom bytes → wrong ack → RST). **Routing** — `ip_send` now sends off-subnet traffic via the gateway (was ARP-ing internet IPs directly) and skips loopback when auto-selecting (was using lo's IP for the TCP pseudo-header). **http_get** waits for the handshake before sending. `parse_ip` + all IP prints normalized to network order (`ifconfig`/DHCP show 10.0.2.15, not reversed). Verified end-to-end via `-object filter-dump` pcap: DHCP→lease, DNS resolves, and `http_get(1.1.1.1:80)` returns a parsed **HTTP 301** with body. (A real HTTP server works; a captive-portal/proxy in some envs returns a non-HTTP page.) |
| v5.7.5 | **GUI stability fixed — the `KERNEL_BASE+3` crash is dead** (and with it the timer/scheduler blocker). Root cause, found with QEMU `-d int` (which, unlike serial `printf`, doesn't perturb the Heisenbug): `irq_common` did the CR3 switch using **rax/rbx as scratch BEFORE `SAVE_REGS`**, so on return the interrupted code resumed with a corrupted RBX (= `KERNEL_BASE`); if it then computed a call/jump from rbx it landed at `KERNEL_BASE+3` → `#UD`. Intermittent because it only bit when the IRQ interrupted an instruction about to use rax/rbx — so it showed up under IRQ load (typing in the GUI terminal, or the timer firing). Fix: move `SAVE_REGS` to the very top of `irq_common` (and `isr_common`), same as the earlier `syscall_entry` fix. Also: boot stack 16 KB→128 KB and moved *above* the page tables (a deep compositor call chain could have overflowed into them); removed the `mpos=`/`.` serial debug spam. **Verified visually** (QEMU SDL + monitor `sendkey`/`screendump`): login → desktop, 12+ terminal commands incl. a full DHCP lease, zero crashes. The timer/scheduler can now be revisited (they crashed via this exact bug). |
| v5.7.6 | **Preemptive scheduler + timer live.** (1) **Timer enabled:** the PIT (ISA IRQ0) is delivered on I/O APIC **pin 2** (QEMU ACPI interrupt-source override), not pin 0 — so the old `irq_unmask(0)` was a no-op and `tick_count` stayed 0. Now `ioapic_redirect_irq(2,32,apic_id)`+`ioapic_unmask_irq(2)` deliver ticks; `sleep()`/uptime work (nyxfetch shows real uptime). (2) **Preemptive round-robin** over kernel threads in `irq_scheduler_tick` (behind `sched_enable()`; dormant by default → keeps the old cooperative behaviour). The compositor runs as the `compositor` process; `init` is a placeholder. Fixed two latent bugs that only fire once you actually `iretq` into a built frame: **(a)** `init_task_stack` set **SS=0x08** (kernel *code* selector) — long-mode `iretq` always pops SS, and loading a code seg #GP's; must be `KERNEL_DS` (0x10). **(b)** the round-robin would switch into the `init` placeholder whose `stack==NULL` (never given a frame) → `iretq` off a null RSP → #GP→#PF→#DF→triple fault; now skips NULL-stack threads. Both found with `qemu -d int,cpu_reset`. (3) **`mtdemo`** shell command: spins up two demo kernel threads that heartbeat ~1 Hz for 3 s then self-retire (`state=0`) so the desktop returns to full speed — the scheduler self-test. Verified visually: `mtdemo` runs both threads concurrently with a stable GUI (counters ~470–515M in 400 ms), then they retire; `ps` lists them. (4) **printf width/flags:** `vprintf` now parses `-`/`0` flags + width (`%-4d`, `%5u`, `%-16s`) — previously the `default` case swallowed `%-4d` **without consuming the arg**, shifting every later arg and garbling `ps`. Backward-compatible (width 0 ⇒ identical output). |
| v5.7.7 | **Preemptive ring-3 processes.** `usertest` spawns two copies of a new `spin.elf` as *scheduled* user processes (`spawn_user_path` → `sched_managed`) that run in ring 3, time-sliced with the GUI, then exit and are reaped — the desktop stays fully responsive (a spin loop would otherwise freeze it). Machinery: (1) the scheduler now switches **user CR3 + TSS RSP0** for `sched_managed` procs and tracks `current_idx` so syscalls resolve per-process; it still skips `init.elf`/the blocking-exec target. (2) **`preempt_disable/enable`** guards `kmalloc`/`kfree` (the freelists aren't reentrant). (3) `SYS_EXIT` from a scheduled proc → `PROC_ZOMBIE` + yield; `reap_zombies()` (a compositor background task) frees the address space/stacks from a safe context — you can't free a process from its own stack. Two latent bugs fixed, both found with `qemu -d int,cpu_reset`: **(a)** user-proc kernel stacks must be the **higher-half** alias — `init_user_task_stack` stored the low kmalloc address, so once the scheduler switched to the user CR3 the low stack was unmapped → #PF→triple fault; store `+KERNEL_BASE` and drop the now-redundant conversion in `switch_to_user_trampoline`. **(b)** a blocking `exec` running while the timer/scheduler is live faulted (#PF, ring 3, kernel CR3): it isn't tracked by `current_idx`, so the "stay on current" path resumed it with the *kernel* CR3 and the user code was unmapped — now `g_user_proc` is resumed in its own CR3 (this had been latent since the timer went live in v5.7.6; fast programs just never took an IRQ mid-run). Verified: `usertest` (2 procs, 8 heartbeats, reaped) **and** `exec /init.elf` (full run) both work with the scheduler active, GUI stable, 0 panics. |
| v5.7.8 | **`wait()` / job control — foreground & background jobs.** `exec <file>` is now built on `spawn_user_path` + a real blocking **`kwait(pid)`**: it spawns the ELF as a scheduled ring-3 process and blocks the shell until the child exits, then prints its exit code (`[exec] PID N exited (code C)`). `spawn <file>` runs the same thing as a **background** job (non-blocking) — it keeps running while the desktop stays interactive; `ps` lists it (with PPID = the shell), and it's reaped when it exits. Mechanism: new **`PROC_BLOCKED`** state + `waiting_for`/`exit_code` fields; `kwait` parks the caller with the classic `cli`-check-then-`sti;hlt` pattern (atomic vs. the child exiting — no lost wakeup on a single core), the scheduler skips blocked threads so the child gets the full CPU, and the child's `SYS_EXIT` calls `wake_waiters()` to set the parent back to `PROC_RUN`. `spawn_user_path` stamps `ppid` so the wakeup finds the right parent. This retires the setjmp/longjmp blocking path — `exec` no longer calls `switch_to_user_process` (that primitive + `g_user_proc` + the trampoline are now unused; removal is a pending cleanup). Verified: `exec /init.elf` runs the full program and returns code 0 with the GUI recovering; `spawn /spin.elf` backgrounds a job that `ps` shows as PID 6 (PPID 4) while the shell stays responsive, then reaps; 0 panics. |
| v5.7.9 | **Legacy blocking-exec path removed.** Pure cleanup, no behaviour change: now that `exec` runs on `spawn`+`kwait` (v5.7.8), the old setjmp/longjmp launcher is dead code, so it's gone — `switch_to_user_process`, `g_user_proc`, `return_from_user_process`, `user_return_ctx`, the `switch_to_user_trampoline` (isr_stubs.asm) and `ku_setjmp`/`ku_longjmp` (switch.asm). `get_cur_proc` now resolves the running process purely via `current_idx`/`get_current_process` (syscalls run with interrupts masked, so it can't change mid-call); `SYS_EXIT` is uniform (every user proc is scheduler-managed → zombie + `wake_waiters` + yield); the scheduler's `g_user_proc` special-case and `reap_user_process`'s `g_user_proc` clear are dropped. One launch path for ring-3 code remains (scheduler `iretq`). Kernel is ~1 KB smaller, 0 warnings. Verified: `exec /init.elf` still runs the full program and returns code 0 (getpid resolves via current_idx → `PID: 5`), `spawn /spin.elf` backgrounds fine, GUI stable, 0 panics. |
| v5.7.10 | **Blocked sleep() + safe kill (timer wait queue).** (1) **`sleep()` no longer busy-waits.** With the scheduler running it now blocks: the caller is marked `PROC_BLOCKED` with a `wake_tick` and yields (`cli`-check-then-`sti;hlt`), so the CPU goes to other threads; `irq_scheduler_tick` runs a wait-queue pass each tick that wakes any sleeper whose `wake_tick` has arrived (distinguished from `kwait` blockers, which use `wake_tick==0`). Before the scheduler exists (early boot) it falls back to an idle `hlt`-loop on `tick_count`. `mtdemo`'s `sleep(400)` now hands the demo threads the full CPU while the shell sleeps. (2) **`kill` is safe on a running process.** `destroy_process` no longer frees a live `sched_managed` proc out from under the scheduler (its ring-3 context may be mid-flight) — it marks it `PROC_ZOMBIE` (+ `wake_waiters`), the scheduler drops it, and `reap_zombies()` frees it. `cmd_kill` refuses kernel threads (`page_directory==NULL`) so you can't nuke init/idle/compositor. Verified: `spawn /spin.elf` then `kill 5` stops it after 3/10 heartbeats (no `SYS_EXIT`), `ps` shows it reaped; `mtdemo`'s blocking sleep returns cleanly (a broken wake would hang the GUI); 0 panics. |
| v5.7.11 | **Shell job control — `jobs` + `wait`.** Completes the job-control set (`exec` foreground, `spawn` background, `kill`): **`jobs`** lists the shell's background jobs (scheduler-managed procs) with pid/ppid/state; **`wait [pid]`** blocks the shell until a specific child (`kwait`) or *all* children (`kwait_all`) exit. `kwait_all` reuses the block/wake machinery — parks the caller `PROC_BLOCKED` with `waiting_for==0` (wait-any) and is woken by any child's `SYS_EXIT` (wake_waiters already matched `waiting_for==0`); zombies count as done and are freed by reap_zombies() once `wait` returns. No struct/scheduler changes — pure additions. Verified: `spawn`×2 → `jobs` lists both running → `wait` blocks until both `exit(0)` (20 heartbeats) then "all background jobs done" → `jobs` shows none (reaped); GUI recovers, 0 panics. |
| v5.7.12 | **Priority scheduling (weighted round-robin).** The scheduler is no longer strict 1-tick round-robin: each proc has a `sched_weight` (ticks per turn; 0⇒1) and a `sched_quantum` countdown. `irq_scheduler_tick` keeps the current thread while its quantum lasts, then picks the next runnable and stamps it a fresh quantum = its weight — so a high-weight proc runs several ticks in a row and gets a proportionally bigger CPU share. The **compositor** is given `SCHED_WEIGHT_GUI` (4), so the desktop stays responsive while background jobs (weight 1) run (~4/(4+N) of the CPU with N jobs). Verified directly: `mtdemo` now gives mtdemoB **weight 3** vs mtdemoA weight 1, and over a 400 ms sample B counted **99.1M vs A's 34.3M — ratio 2.89 ≈ 3.0**, exactly tracking the weights. No regression: `exec /init.elf` runs and exits code 0, GUI stable, 0 panics. (Struct change → clean rebuild.) |
| v5.7.13 | **VFS re-entrancy audit + guard.** Audit of shared FS state under preemption found the dominant hazard: EXT2 uses **one global scratch buffer** (`block_buf` in ext2.c) across a whole operation, and the VFS **node pool / free-list** (`alloc_node`/`free_node`) aren't reentrant. User FS syscalls already run with interrupts masked (atomic), but a *kernel-context* FS caller — a shell command / file-manager action on the compositor thread — runs with interrupts on and can be preempted mid-operation; a scheduled process's FS syscall would then trample the same buffer/pool. (Latent today: `exec` blocks the compositor while its child does I/O, and `spawn`ed demos do no FS — but it'd bite the moment a background job touches the FS.) Fix: a coarse single-core "FS lock" via `preempt_disable` — `alloc_node`/`free_node` guard the pool, and seven thin wrappers (`fs_read_file`/`fs_write_file`/`fs_readdir`/…) on the mount function-pointers run each EXT2 op with preemption off, so ext2.c is untouched and every VFS→EXT2 path is serialized. Noted (not fixed, not crash-risk): the `ufd` table is shared across procs but safe under atomic syscalls (fds aren't per-process isolated); `current_dir` is a single global CWD; `auth.c` + the boot self-test call ext2 directly but only pre-scheduler (boot/login). Verified: boot mount/write-test/auth, `ls /mnt` (ext2 readdir wrapper), and `exec /init.elf` file I/O all work, 0 panics. |
| v5.7.14 | **Per-process file descriptors.** The `ufd` table (opaque fd → internal VFS handle + offset) moved from three **global** arrays in syscall.c into `process_t` — one table per process. `ufd_alloc`/`lookup`/`release`/`offset_of` now index the current process's table (`get_cur_proc()`; syscalls run with interrupts masked so `current_idx` is stable). fds are now **isolated** (a process can't name another's fd) and **freed with the process**: `close_proc_fds()` runs on reap (`reap_zombies`/`reap_user_process`), closing anything left open so a careless/crashed process can't leak VFS handles or exhaust the table. Verified with a new `fdleak.elf` that opens `/init.elf` and exits *without* closing: the kernel logs `[reap] force-closed leaked fd(s)`, and running it repeatedly never exhausts fds (each run gets its own fresh table, PID 5 then 6). init.elf's normal open/read/write/close I/O still works. Deferred (documented): per-process **CWD** — `current_dir` stays global because relative paths are shell-only (user procs use absolute paths), there's one shell, and it feeds the core `resolve_path`; low value, not worth the risk yet. |
| v5.7.15 | **Idle desktop yields CPU + `nice`/`renice`.** (1) The compositor no longer busy-polls: at the top of its loop, when nothing is happening — no key queued (`kbd_head==kbd_tail`), no mouse button, the pointer unmoved, no drag/resize/menu active — it `sleep(5)`s instead of spinning. So an idle desktop hands the CPU to background jobs (or lets it idle) rather than burning its weight-4 slice; any activity skips the sleep, so interactivity is unchanged (≤5 ms wakeup). Measured: a background `spin.elf` (10 heartbeats) ran at ~full CPU with the desktop idle, vs the ~⅕ it'd get from the weight-4 compositor busy-polling. (2) **`nice <weight> <file>`** spawns a background job at a chosen scheduler weight; **`renice <pid> <weight>`** changes a running process's weight (1..64) — exposing the v5.7.12 weighted round-robin from the shell. Verified: `nice 8 /spin.elf` → PID 6 weight 8, `renice 6 2` → weight 2, GUI responsive throughout, 0 panics. |
| v5.7.16 | **File manager: real drag-and-drop into folders.** The FM already had copy/paste (ctx-menu + Ctrl-C/X/V), a context menu, search, rename, and new file/folder — but its **drag-and-drop was a no-op**: on drop it always targeted the *current* directory (`dst = cwd/basename`), so dragging a file within a folder computed `dst == src` and did nothing. Fixed: a new `fileman_row_at()` maps the drop point to the row under the cursor, so dropping a file **onto a folder row moves/copies it into that folder** (`dst = targetfolder/basename`). Plain drag = **move** (`vfs_rename`), Ctrl-drag = **copy** (`vfs_cp`) — the drag ghost shows MOVE/COPY live. Also: pasting a copied file into the same directory now makes a unique `<name>_copy` instead of failing on the name collision. Verified: FM opens and lists the live FS (incl. a shell-created `/box`); the drop backend (`vfs_cp`/`vfs_rename` into a subfolder) confirmed via the shell's `cp /f1 /box/…` + `mv /f1 /box/…`; 0 panics. |
| v5.7.17 | **Networking: loopback + ICMP echo replies + a real `ping`.** Three gaps closed so NyxOS behaves like a host on the network. (1) **Loopback delivery** — packets addressed to one of our own IPs (`127.0.0.0/8` or a NIC's own address) no longer try to ARP or hit the wire; `ip_send` copies the finished IP packet into a small ring that `ip_loopback_poll()` (drained by `kernel_poll_net`) re-injects into `ip_handle_packet`, so `ping 127.0.0.1` works entirely in-guest. A handler may enqueue a reply mid-drain (echo request→reply); the same pass picks it up, so a loopback ping resolves in one poll (no deep recursion). (2) **ICMP echo responder** — `icmp_handle_packet` now answers echo *requests* (type 8) with a reply (type 0), reusing the payload/id/seq — NyxOS is pingable. (3) **Real `ping` client** — `icmp_ping` was a busy-wait with a fixed id and no output; it now uses a random ident, per-packet seq, matches replies by id+seq, times RTT off `get_ticks()`, and prints iputils-style lines + a summary (`N transmitted, M received, P% loss`; `rtt min/avg/max`). Also fixed a **byte-order bug** in the numeric-IP parser (and the `lo` literal): IPs are stored network-order (first octet = low byte) everywhere else, but `ping`'s parser built them high-byte-first — so it printed `127.0.0.1` as `1.0.0.127` and a numeric `ping <ip>` aimed at a byte-reversed address. Verified in QEMU: `ping 127.0.0.1` → 4/4 replies, `rtt min/avg/max = 0/0/0 ms`, 0% loss (exercises loopback + responder + reply-match + RTT + stats); `ifconfig` now shows `lo … IP 127.0.0.1`; DHCP (10.0.2.15) + ARP still resolve; `ping 10.0.2.2`/`8.8.8.8` send correctly but time out cleanly (100% loss, graceful, no hang) — QEMU slirp's gateway doesn't answer ICMP echo, a known emulator limitation, not a stack bug. 0 panics. |
| v5.7.18 | **TCP retransmission (RTO) + robust multi-segment receive.** TCP was fire-and-forget: `send_segment` transmitted once, a lost segment was never resent, and an incoming ACK of our data fell through the state machine unhandled. Now each connection buffers its one outstanding sequence-consuming segment (SYN/FIN/data) verbatim; **`tcp_tick()`** — run from `kernel_poll_net` (every network poll; never an IRQ, since it does ip_send/kmalloc) — resends it once the RTO elapses with **exponential backoff** (300 ms → ×2 → 2400 ms cap) and, after `TCP_MAX_RETRIES` (5), resets the connection. Incoming ACKs are now processed: a cumulative ack past the buffered segment clears it (stops the timer). A testing hook — `tcpdrop <n>` / `tcp_debug_drop()` — silently drops the next N outbound segments (kept armed for retransmit) so the RTO path is exercisable in-guest. Separately, **multi-segment receive** was made robust: `http_get`'s receive/handshake loops were rewritten from a fixed iteration count (a normal inter-segment gap tripped the old counter after one partial segment → truncated/empty bodies) to `get_ticks()`-based timeouts with Content-Length / FIN completion, so large streamed responses arrive whole; `cmd_tcptest` now waits for the handshake and sends the correct `Host:` header (and dumps raw bytes for diagnosis). Verified over real slirp TCP: a genuinely slow SYN to neverssl.com (34.223.124.45) was **recovered by two retransmits** (retry 1 @600 ms, retry 2 @1200 ms — backoff visible) → handshake established → full `HTTP/1.1 403` response (all headers + 239-byte body) received and parsed; `tcpdrop 1` + connect shows the dropped SYN retransmitted and the connection still completing; loopback `ping` unaffected; 0 panics. (Found en route: the host's ISP returns a non-HTTP "Contenido bloqueado" page for some domains — an environment artifact, not a stack bug; DNS + handshake + multi-segment receive all work.) |
| v5.7.19 | **TCP passive open (`listen`/`accept`) + in-guest loopback TCP.** TCP was client-only — an inbound SYN got an RST. Now `tcp_listen(port)` reserves a LISTEN socket; a pure inbound SYN to that port spawns a **child** connection (SYN_RCVD → SYN-ACK), the client's ACK promotes it to ESTABLISHED, and `tcp_accept(listen_id)` hands out the established child while the listener keeps listening. `tcp_connect` is now loopback-aware — a `127.0.0.0/8` destination sources from `lo` and needs no NIC. Combined with the v5.7.17 loopback ring, the whole TCP path — 3-way handshake, bidirectional data, cumulative ACKs, and v5.7.18 retransmission — is now testable **entirely in-guest** with zero external dependency. New `tcploop [drop]` self-test does exactly that: `tcp_listen` + `tcp_connect(127.0.0.1)`, drives the handshake, then sends a message each way and checks the bytes. Also hardened the no-connection path to never answer an RST with an RST (over loopback that would ping-pong forever). Verified: `tcploop` → handshake (client conn 1 ↔ server child 2), server & client each receive the exact payload → **PASS**; `tcploop 1` drops the client's 37-byte data segment (`TX dropped seq=1001 len=37`), the RTO retransmits it (`retransmit conn 1 retry 1`), the server still receives it and the echo completes → **PASS** — all over 127.0.0.1, no NIC, 0 panics. (Note: NIC-side inbound `listen` isn't fully wired yet — a child's local IP defaults to lo, so loopback is the supported server path today; wiring the received dst IP through would enable NIC servers.) |
| v5.7.20 | **NIC-side TCP listen — NyxOS serves HTTP to real clients.** v5.7.19's passive open only worked over loopback: a server child took its local IP from `local_ip`, which `tcp_handle_packet` computed as the *first* interface (always `lo`), so a child accepted on the NIC built its SYN-ACK with a 127.0.0.1 pseudo-header → wrong TCP checksum → slirp/the peer dropped it. Fix: thread the received packet's **destination IP** (the address the peer actually targeted) through `ip_handle_packet` → `tcp_handle_packet`, and source the child from it — a NIC child now checksums against the NIC IP, a loopback child against 127.0.0.1. A second bug, pinned down from a `filter-dump` pcap: a client that FINs right after its request (curl half-closes) moves the child to **CLOSE_WAIT** before the server replies, but `tcp_send`/`tcp_close` only acted in ESTABLISHED — so the response was silently dropped (`tcp_send` returned −1, no data segment on the wire). Now both accept CLOSE_WAIT (send the reply, then FIN → LAST_ACK) — the standard server half-close. New `tcpserve [port]` accepts one connection, prints the request, and returns a small HTTP/1.1 200. Verified end-to-end with QEMU `hostfwd=tcp::8080-:80`: `curl http://127.0.0.1:8080/` **from the host** → NyxOS accepts the inbound connection, reads the 78-byte `curl/8.19.0` GET, and the host receives `HTTP/1.1 200 OK` / `Content-Length: 21` / `Hello from NyxOS TCP!`. The pcap shows a clean SYN → SYN-ACK(seq=1000) → ACK → request(78) → ACK → response → FIN exchange with correct seq/ack. Loopback `tcploop` still PASS; 0 panics. |
| v5.7.21 | **Per-shell CWD + full-path `pwd`/prompt.** The current directory was a single global `current_dir` in vfs.c, shared by every Terminal window — `cd` in one moved them all. Now each Terminal owns its directory: `terminal_win_t` gained a `cwd` node (opaque `void*`), initialised to `/` on creation, swapped into the VFS (`vfs_setcwd_node`) right before `execute_command` and saved back (`vfs_getcwd_node`) after — so `cd` in one shell never disturbs another, and the mechanism is single-threaded-safe (the compositor runs one command at a time). `vfs_getcwd()` was also rewritten to build the **full absolute path** by walking each node's `parent` pointer to the root, instead of returning just the leaf name — so `pwd` and the prompt now show `/home/user`, not `user`. The Terminal prompt is rebuilt from its own CWD after every command. Verified in QEMU: a single shell walks `nyx:/$` → `cd home` → `nyx:/home$` (`pwd` → `/home`) → `ls` → `user/` → `cd user` → `nyx:/home/user$` (`pwd` → `/home/user`); relative `cd`/`ls` resolve against the shell's CWD; 0 panics. (Per-terminal independence is structural — every `terminal_create_ctx` starts at `/` and swaps its own `cwd` — but wasn't shown live because opening a 2nd GUI terminal needs the monitor mouse, which QEMU drives unreliably. User-*process* CWD stays deferred: no `chdir` syscall, ring-3 code uses absolute paths.) |
| v5.7.22 | **SMP: multi-core bringup verified + exposed (`cpus`).** The AP-bringup scaffolding — INIT-SIPI-SIPI → a real-mode trampoline that climbs through protected to long mode and calls `ap_main` — already brought secondary cores up, but silently. This makes it visible and adds per-core self-verification. Fixed a latent triple-fault first: `ap_main` read the core's APIC id from the **Local APIC MMIO** (`0xFEE00000`), but an AP runs on the plain kernel PML4 which doesn't map that device page → `#PF` with no AP IDT → triple fault (QEMU exited under `-no-reboot`, boot froze at "3 AP(s) found"); now the AP reads its id via **CPUID(1).EBX[31:24]** — an instruction, no memory access. Each AP records its self-read APIC id; the BSP is always recorded as CPU 0 so a single-core machine reports correctly too. New `cpus` command prints the topology. Verified: `qemu -smp 4` → `[SMP] 4 CPU(s) online`, and `cpus` shows CPU 0 (BSP) + APs 1–3 with **assigned APIC id == self-read APIC id** (0/0, 1/1, 2/2, 3/3) — proof each is a distinct core executing kernel C; a default single-core boot shows just the BSP; 0 panics. (APs park in `cli; hlt`; actually running threads on them — per-CPU scheduling, reschedule IPIs, TLB shootdown — is the next, much larger step.) |
| v5.7.23 | **Demand paging + copy-on-write (serviced from the #PF handler).** The page-fault handler was panic-only; now `isr_handler` calls `vm_handle_fault(cr2, err)` first. Two OS-available PTE marker bits drive it: **`PTE_DEMAND`** (bit 9) — a not-present PTE that, on first touch (`err` bit0==0), allocates a zeroed page and maps it present+writable; **`PTE_COW`** (bit 10) — a present, read-only PTE that, on a write fault (`err` bits0&1), gets a private writable copy (the shared page is left untouched). A new `pte_ptr(pml4, virt, create)` walks/creates the leaf PTE; `vm_map_demand`/`vm_map_cow`/`vm_unmap` set it up. **Enabled `CR0.WP`** — without it, ring-0 writes to read-only pages don't fault, so the COW write silently hit the shared page (the first run showed "cow faults taken: 0, original page BAD shared!"); with WP the supervisor write traps. The ELF loader is unaffected: it copies segments through the writable identity mapping (the physical address), not the read-only user VA. New `cowtest` self-test. Verified in QEMU: demand → first read returns 0 (zeroed), then writes stick (1 demand fault); COW → read sees the shared value, a write makes `0xBBBB…` while the original stays `0xAAAA…` (1 cow fault) → **PASS**; `usertest` still spawns ring-3 procs (ELF loader OK under WP); 0 panics. (Kernel-space today via the self-test; wiring it into lazy `sbrk`/`fork` is future work.) |
| v5.7.24 | **GitHub Wiki + clean up.** Live wiki at `https://github.com/kazah-png/nyx-os/wiki` with 16 comprehensive markdown pages covering all kernel subsystems: Home, Architecture, Building, Boot Process, Memory Management, Process Management, Networking Stack, GUI Subsystem, Filesystem, Syscalls, SMP, Drivers, Shell, Userspace, Security, Version History. Pages use wiki-style `[[Page Name]]` links with sidebar navigation. Removed local `wiki/` directory from repo (redundant with live GitHub Wiki). README badge updated to point to GitHub Wiki. |
| v5.7.25 | **Real `fork()` — copy-on-write process clone (the VM capstone).** A ring-3 process can now `fork()` (`SYS_FORK`, 10): the child gets a COW copy of the parent's address space and resumes at the same instruction with `fork()==0`, while the parent gets the child's pid — both then round-robin preemptively. Machinery: **(1) physical page refcounting** (`memory.c`): `alloc_page` sets refcount 1, `page_incref` shares a frame, `free_page` now decrements and only frees the frame at the last reference — so COW-shared pages survive one owner exiting. **(2) `clone_page_directory_cow`** walks the parent's user half (PML4[0..510]); each writable leaf is downgraded to **read-only + `PTE_COW`** in *both* parent and child and its refcount bumped, read-only pages (code) are shared as-is — the first writer on either side takes a private copy via the #PF handler. **(3) `vm_handle_fault` reworked**: a user fault now resolves the address space from the *running process's* `page_directory` (the scheduler tracks it by `current_idx`, not the lagging global `current_pml4`), and the COW copy is refcount-aware (sole owner → un-COW in place; else copy + drop the shared reference). **(4) `isr_common` restores the faulting CR3** (`mov r15,cr3` / `mov cr3,r15` around the handler): a recoverable ring-3 fault must `iretq` back on the process's own CR3, not the kernel CR3 the handler switches to. **(5) `do_fork`** builds the child's ring-3 resume frame from the parent's saved syscall register frame (exposed via a new `syscall_frame_ptr` in isr_stubs.asm) with `rax=0`, clones the process_t, and marks both parent and child `sched_managed`. Two bugs fixed in-flight: the page-table walker `pte_ptr` created intermediate tables **without `PAGE_USER`**, so a forked child's user pages sat behind supervisor-only intermediates and ring 3 couldn't even fetch its own code (`#PF` present+user+instr-fetch at its entry) — now set USER on intermediates (the leaf still gates access), matching `map_pml4`; and `vm_map_cow` now `page_incref`s the mapped frame so a single-alloc COW page isn't mistaken for a sole-owner and written in place. Verified in QEMU (`exec /init.elf`): **child** prints `fork()=0, getpid=6, cow_test=200 (own copy)` and **parent** prints `fork()=6 (child pid), getpid=5, cow_test=100 (unchanged)` — distinct pids and a proven COW split (same virtual address, divergent values after the write); the `cowtest` self-test still **PASS**; 0 panics. |
| v5.8.0 | **Nyx C language runtime + first `.nyx` program on NyxOS.** Nyx C is a Go/Zig-inspired typed subset of C that transpiles to C via `nyxc` and links against the `nyxrt` runtime. Adds: `user/nyxrt.h` — runtime types (`nyx_str`, `nyx_slice`, `nyx_result`), `__nyx_syscall6` ABI, and string-interpolation helpers (`__nyx_fmt_begin`/`_str`/`_i64`); `user/nyxrt.c` — runtime implementation; `user/hello.nyx` — first Nyx C program with syscall declarations and string interpolation; `user/hello_nyx.c` — transpiled C output. Build: `NYXCFLAGS` rule in `kernel/makefile`, `hello_nyx.elf` linked with crt0+nyxrt, embedded in initramfs. Verified: `exec /hello_nyx.elf` prints `"hola desde nyx c! pid=5"` in terminal. |
| v5.8.1 | **`waitpid()` — a parent reaps its children from ring 3.** Completes the fork story (v5.7.25): `SYS_WAITPID` (11) lets a ring-3 parent block until a child exits and collect its exit code (`long waitpid(int pid, int* status)`; `pid<=0` waits for any child). `do_waitpid` (process.c) finds the caller's child, and when it's a zombie copies out its exit code, frees its address space + kernel stack + `process_t`, and swap-removes it from the table (keeping `current_idx` valid). The kernel side is deliberately **non-blocking** — it returns `-2` (EAGAIN) while the child still runs and the userspace `waitpid()` wrapper spins on that, because a true sleeping wait would `sti;hlt` mid-syscall and the single shared syscall stack (`kernel_rsp`) would be clobbered by the child's own syscalls (per-process syscall stacks are the future fix; today syscalls are atomic under `SF_MASK`, so the poll is safe). To stop the compositor's `reap_zombies()` from collecting a child before its parent does, that reaper now **skips zombies whose parent is a live user process** — only orphans and kernel-thread-parented background jobs (`spawn`) are auto-reaped; a user parent's children wait for its `waitpid`. Verified in QEMU (`exec /init.elf`): after the COW fork, the child `exit(123)`s and the parent prints `waitpid(6) -> reaped pid 6, child exit code = 123`; `ps` afterwards shows no leaked zombie; `cowtest` still **PASS**; 0 panics. |
| v5.8.2 | **Per-process syscall stacks → `waitpid()` truly blocks.** The syscall path used a single shared kernel stack (`kernel_rsp`, set once at boot) plus `user_cr3`/`user_rsp` globals — fine while syscalls ran atomically (IF masked by `SF_MASK`), but a syscall that *blocks* (`sti;hlt`) can be preempted, and another process's syscall would then clobber the shared stack. Now `sched_target` points `kernel_rsp` at each process's **own** kernel stack (the same higher-half alias as its TSS RSP0), so a process parked mid-syscall keeps its frame on its own stack. With that, `waitpid()` (v5.8.1) becomes a TRUE block: `do_waitpid` parks the caller `PROC_BLOCKED` + `sti;hlt` (woken by the child's `SYS_EXIT` via `wake_waiters`) instead of the old userspace poll. Two subtleties handled: **(1)** a process blocked in ring 0 must resume on the **kernel CR3**, not its user CR3 — `-mcmodel=large` kernel code runs at low link addresses only mapped in the kernel PML4, so resuming on the user CR3 `#PF`'d on `do_waitpid`'s own code (RIP=`0x108843`, ring 0, not-present instruction fetch); a new `process_t.blocked_in_kernel` flag makes the scheduler choose the kernel CR3 for such procs (the process switches back to its user CR3 itself when the syscall returns). **(2)** the child's syscalls overwrite the shared `user_cr3`/`user_rsp` globals while we sleep, so `do_waitpid` saves them on entry and restores them before returning, so the asm syscall-return path iretq's back into the right address space. Verified (`exec /init.elf`): the parent blocks in `waitpid` while the child runs **three** `printf` syscalls (`working 1/3..3/3`) + `exit(123)` on its own stack, then wakes and collects `child exit code = 123` — the old shared stack would have corrupted the parent's parked frame. Two back-to-back `exec` runs (PIDs 5 & 7 both exit 0) + `cowtest` **PASS**; 0 panics. (Foundation for future blocking syscalls — pipe/keyboard reads.) |
| v5.8.3 | **Anonymous pipes + blocking `read()` — real cross-process IPC.** `pipe(int fds[2])` (`SYS_PIPE`, 12) returns a read end (`fds[0]`) and a write end (`fds[1]`) onto a kernel ring buffer (`pipe.c`, 16 pipes × 4 KB). A `read()` on an empty pipe **blocks** until a writer writes, or returns `0` (EOF) once all writers have closed; `write()` copies into the ring and wakes a parked reader; each end is **reference-counted** so `fork()` can share it and EOF fires only when the last writer closes. The read block reuses the v5.8.2 machinery — `blocked_in_kernel` (resume on the kernel CR3) + `user_cr3`/`user_rsp` save/restore around the `sti;hlt` — and the writer wakes the reader by marking it `PROC_RUN`. Integration is via the existing per-process fd table: a pipe fd is stored in `ufd_handle` with a high `UFD_PIPE_FLAG` bit (low bits = `pipe_id<<1 | end`), so `SYS_READ`/`WRITE`/`CLOSE` route to the pipe layer instead of the VFS; `do_fork` now inherits the parent's **pipe** fds (with a refcount bump — VFS fds still aren't, as their handles aren't refcounted), and `close_proc_fds` drops pipe refs on exit. Verified (`exec /init.elf`): after `pipe()`+`fork()`, the parent `write`s 27 bytes and the child `read`s them back — `read 27 bytes from pipe: "hello from parent via pipe!"` — then `waitpid` reaps the child; two runs, VFS file I/O + `cowtest` regressions all clean, 0 panics. (Enables shell pipelines `a | b`; today one reader per pipe, non-blocking writes.) |
| v5.8.4 | **`execve()` — replace a process image (fork→exec→wait complete).** Unlike `SYS_EXEC` (9, which *spawns* a new process), `execve(path, argv, envp)` (`SYS_EXECVE`, 13) replaces the **calling** process's image in place: same pid, same open fds, new address space. `elf_load` was refactored to split out `elf_load_image` (load an ELF into a fresh page directory — segments with W^X flags + a user stack — returning pd/entry/stack-top/brk; also plugs a pre-existing leak on the segment-alloc failure path). `do_execve` (process.c) then loads the new image, swaps `page_directory` on the current `process_t` and frees the old one (COW-refcount aware — pages still shared with a forked relative survive), and **rewrites this syscall's saved user frame** (via `syscall_frame_ptr`, the same hook `do_fork` uses): zero the GPRs, set RIP = new entry + RFLAGS = 0x202, and point `user_rsp`/`user_cr3` at the new stack and address space — so the `syscall` return path iretq's straight into the new program. crt0 hardcodes `argc=0`, so no argv frame is built yet (argv/envp are accepted but not threaded onto the new stack). On failure the caller is left intact and gets -1. Verified (`exec /init.elf`): the child `fork`s then `execve("/hello.elf", …)` — its own code is discarded, hello.elf runs in its place and prints its two-line banner + `exit(42)`, and the parent's `waitpid` collects `42`. With this, one `init.elf` run exercises the whole model — file I/O, `fork`+COW, blocking `waitpid`, `pipe`, and `execve` — plus `cowtest` **PASS**; 0 panics. (fork+execve+wait+pipe = the pieces for a real userspace shell.) |
| v5.8.5 | **`dup2()` — fd redirection (the shell-pipeline primitive).** `dup2(oldfd, newfd)` (`SYS_DUP2`, 14) makes `newfd` refer to the same pipe end as `oldfd`, closing whatever `newfd` held — so `dup2(pipefd[1], 1)` sends a process's stdout into a pipe, which is exactly how `a \| b` wires two processes together. Structural change that enables it: the per-process fd table was **re-indexed by the fd itself** (slots 0..PROC_MAX_FDS-1, previously index = fd−3, so fds 0-2 *couldn't exist* in the table); `open()`/`pipe()` still allocate from `UFD_BASE` (3) up, leaving 0-2 free unless dup2'd onto. `SYS_WRITE` now checks the fd table **first** and only falls back to the console for an *empty* fd 1/2 slot — a redirected stdout routes to the pipe, a normal one still prints; `SYS_READ` picks up a dup2'd fd 0 automatically (lookup-first already). dup2 is **pipe-fds only** for now: pipe ends are reference-counted (each duplicate fd takes a ref via `pipe_incref`; exit's `close_proc_fds` drops each), while VFS handles aren't refcounted — two fds on one handle would double-`vfs_close` on reap → -1. Verified (`exec /init.elf`): child `dup2(pipe_w, 1)` + `write(1, "stdout was redirected!")` → the bytes land in the **pipe**, parent reads them back (22 bytes); all prior demos pass in the same run despite the re-index — file I/O still gets `fd=3`, fork+`waitpid` (123), pipe (27 bytes), `execve` (42) — two exec runs + `cowtest` **PASS**, 0 panics. (With fork+execve+waitpid+pipe+dup2 complete, a real userspace shell with pipelines is now buildable.) |
| v5.8.6 | **`execve()` argv passing — programs receive real command-line arguments.** `execve(path, argv, envp)` now delivers `argv` to the new program via the standard **SysV entry-stack layout**: the kernel copies each string onto the top of the new program's stack, builds `[argc][argv[0..n-1]][NULL][envp NULL]` below them (entry RSP 16-byte aligned), and `crt0` reads `argc = [rsp]`, `argv = rsp+8` before calling `main(argc, argv)`. Because crt0 now reads the stack on EVERY launch, `elf_load_image` (and `create_user_process`'s fallback stack path) write an **empty frame** (`argc=0`, NULL argv/envp, 32 bytes) at the stack top for plain spawns — so `exec`/`spawn`/boot-init all keep working, just with `main(0, …)` as before. Plumbing: the `SYS_EXECVE` case copies the caller's argv (a NULL-terminated user array of user string pointers, max 8 × 63 chars) into kernel buffers **before** `do_execve` destroys the old address space; `do_execve(data, size, kargv, argc)` then points `user_cr3` at the new pd and builds the frame through `copy_to_user` (now exported from syscall.c — it translates via `user_cr3`, so the writes land in the fresh stack page through the identity map); frame `rdi`/`rsi` = argc/argv as a courtesy for register-based entry code. New test target `user/args.elf` prints its argv and exits with argc. Verified (`exec /init.elf`): child `execve("/args.elf", {"args","uno","dos","tres"})` → prints `argc=4` + all four strings, parent's `waitpid` gets status **4** (independent confirmation); the crt0 change is regression-tested across every program type — init.elf's full suite (file I/O, fork+`waitpid` 123, pipe 27B, execve 42, dup2 redirect) + `hello_nyx.elf` (Nyx C, same crt0: `hola desde nyx c!`) + `cowtest` **PASS**, 0 panics. (envp still ignored; a shell can now run `prog arg1 arg2`.) |
| v5.8.7 | **A userspace shell with pipelines — the process-model capstone, zero kernel changes.** New `user/sh.c` → `/sh.elf`: a ring-3 shell built purely on the syscall surface grown in v5.7.25→v5.8.6 — `fork()` + `execve(argv)` + `waitpid()` + `pipe()` + `dup2()`. It parses a command line into up to 4 stages joined by `\|`, fork+execve's each stage with its stdin/stdout wired to the neighbouring pipes via `dup2` (closing the raw fds promptly so EOF fires when the last writer exits), resolves bare names (`echo` → `/echo.elf`), and `waitpid`s every stage, reporting non-zero exit statuses. Two coreutils to pipe between: `user/echo.c` (argv → stdout) and `user/upper.c` (stdin → uppercase → stdout until EOF) — and since libc's `putchar`/`puts` write via `write(1,…)`, *any* C program's printf is pipeline-capturable. Modes: `sh -c "line"`, or a built-in demo script when run bare (interactive stdin needs kernel keyboard→fd0 routing — future work). Verified (`exec /sh.elf`): `echo hello…` prints; **`echo pipelines are working on nyxos \| upper` → `PIPELINES ARE WORKING ON NYXOS`** — a real two-process pipeline through a kernel pipe, wired entirely from ring 3; `args one two three` prints its argv and sh reports `exited with status 4`. Full `init.elf` regression (file I/O, fork+waitpid 123, pipe 27B, execve 42, dup2 redirect, argv 4) + two sh runs + `cowtest` **PASS**, 0 panics. The kernel didn't change — proof the process API is complete. |
| v5.8.8 | **Interactive stdin — a live shell you type into.** `read(0, …)` on an EMPTY fd-0 slot now reads the **keyboard** with a canonical line discipline (`stdin_read_line`, syscall.c): it drains the IRQ-fed ASCII ring via `getchar_poll()`, **echoes** each key (`putchar` mirrors to the terminal capture hook + serial), handles backspace, and returns the line when Enter arrives. Blocking while the ring is empty reuses the v5.8.2 mid-syscall discipline (`sti;hlt` a timeslice at a time — the timer keeps re-scheduling us, so no keyboard-IRQ wakeup hook is needed yet — with `blocked_in_kernel` so the scheduler resumes on the kernel CR3, and the shared `user_cr3`/`user_rsp` globals saved/restored around the loop). No contention: the compositor, the ring's usual consumer, is parked in `kwait()` while a foreground process runs. `SYS_READ` was restructured lookup-first so a `dup2`'d fd 0 (a pipeline's read end) still routes to the pipe, while a bare fd 0 falls through to stdin. `sh` (`user/sh.c`) becomes a real **REPL** (v0.2): a `sh$ ` prompt, `read(0)` a line, builtins `exit`/`demo`, else run the pipeline — `sh -c` still works. Verified live in QEMU (`exec /sh.elf`, keystrokes via the monitor): typed `demo` runs the canned script, `echo hola nyx` → `hola nyx`, `echo … | upper` still pipelines, `exit` → `sh: bye`; the full `init.elf` regression (fork+waitpid 123, pipe, execve 42, dup2, argv 4) + `cowtest` **PASS**, 0 panics. (Line editing is echo+backspace; job control / arrow-key history are future work.) |
| v5.8.9 | **Lazy `sbrk` — the demand-paged heap (last VM roadmap item).** `SYS_SBRK` no longer eagerly allocates + maps pages as the break grows — it just moves `program_break` (and can shrink it, clamped at `heap_start`). Pages materialise on **first touch**: a USER not-present fault (`err` bit2 set, bit0 clear) whose CR2 lies in the process's heap window **[`heap_start`, `program_break`)** is serviced by `vm_handle_fault` (alloc a zeroed page, `map_page_dir` P\|W\|U\|NX) — checked BEFORE the normal `pte_ptr` walk, since a brand-new heap page has no page-table entry or intermediate tables yet. New `process_t.heap_start` = the initial break, set at ELF load and `execve`, inherited across `fork`. So a big `malloc` that's only partly written costs only the pages actually used. It slots cleanly onto the v5.7.25 VM work: user faults already resolve to `get_current_process()->page_directory`, and `isr_common` already restores the faulting CR3 so the process resumes on its own address space with the new page mapped. Verified (`exec /init.elf`): `malloc(8000)` grows the break `0x16000 → 0x19000` (+12288 = 3 pages) **lazily**, and the pattern written across all 8000 bytes reads back **intact** — plus the full process suite (fork+waitpid 123, pipe, execve 42, dup2, argv 4) and `cowtest` still **PASS**, 0 panics. (Lost twice to the early working-tree resets; now landed. Foundation for a future `mmap`.) |
| v5.8.10 | **Coreutils (`cat`/`wc`/`ls`) + shell background jobs (`&`).** Three new ring-3 programs on the existing syscall surface — `user/cat.c` (files or stdin → stdout), `user/wc.c` (lines/words/bytes of stdin or files), `user/ls.c` (directory listing) — plus job control in `sh`. `ls` needed a new enumeration syscall: **`SYS_GETDENTS` (15)** runs the whole `vfs_open`/`vfs_readdir`/`vfs_close` cycle in the kernel and copies up to N fixed 68-byte `{char name[64]; u32 type}` records into a user buffer (ring 3 never holds a raw VFS handle). A lazy-`sbrk` interaction surfaced and is handled: `copy_to_user` walks the *user* page tables and can't fault a not-yet-touched heap page in, so `ls` `memset`s its `malloc`'d buffer first (every page resident) and `getdents` returns the count-so-far rather than -1 if a copy ever fails. **Background jobs:** a trailing `&` makes `sh` fork the pipeline and *not* wait — it parks the pids and prints `[bg] pid N`, then sweeps them non-blocking at each prompt via the new **`waitpid(…, WNOHANG)`** (`do_waitpid` gained an `options` arg; the `SYS_WAITPID` case threads `a3`; WNOHANG returns 0 instead of blocking when a child exists but hasn't exited). The shell reaps its own bg zombies — `reap_zombies()` only frees procs whose parent has gone — so `reap_bg()` prints `[done] pid N (status S)` as each finishes. Verified (`exec /init.elf` + `exec /sh.elf`): `ls /` lists the dirs (trailing `/`) and all 12 `.elf`s incl. `cat`/`ls`/`wc`; `cat welcome.txt | wc` → `1 4 26`; the WNOHANG probe polls 61× while a child runs then reaps status 7; in the shell `echo … | upper &` → `[bg] pid 25`, runs in the background, and both stages are reaped `[done] pid 24/25` at the next prompt. Full process suite (fork+waitpid 123, pipe, execve 42, dup2, argv 4) + `cowtest` **PASS**, 0 panics. (`welcome.txt` is 26 B — boot-time `vfs.c` writes it with two offset-less `vfs_write`s, a pre-existing quirk; `wc`'s `1 4 26` is correct for the real file.) |
| v5.8.11 | **Signals — `kill`/`signal`/handlers + Ctrl-C.** A POSIX-style signal subset in a new `kernel/signal.c`: **`SYS_KILL` (16)**, **`SYS_SIGNAL` (17)**, **`SYS_SIGRETURN` (18)**. Model: a signal is *posted* (set a bit in the target's `sig_pending`) and *delivered* at return-to-ring-3 by `signal_dispatch`, which the syscall entry stub (isr_stubs.asm) calls with the saved user frame after finalizing it. Delivery: **SIG_IGN** drops it; **SIG_DFL** terminates (exit code `128+signo`, shell convention — or ignores for SIGCHLD/SIGCONT); a **user handler** is entered by saving the interrupted context into `process_t.sig_saved`, pushing a return trampoline (`__sigreturn` in crt0.asm) onto the user stack, and rewriting the frame (RIP=handler, RDI=signo, user_rsp=adjusted) — the handler returns through the trampoline into `SYS_SIGRETURN`, which restores the saved context so the process resumes where it was interrupted. `process_t` gains `sig_pending`/`sig_mask`/`sig_active`/`sig_handlers[NSIG]`/`sig_trampoline`/`sig_saved[18]`; `fork` inherits dispositions, `execve` resets them (the old handler addresses die with the image). **Ctrl-C**: the keyboard IRQ (`ctrl_pressed` + `c`) posts SIGINT to the foreground process (`g_foreground_pid`, set by `cmd_exec`); `stdin_read_line` is now signal-aware (returns `-EINTR` when a signal is pending) and `do_kill` wakes a blocked target, so a signal actually interrupts a blocking `read(0)`. Verified (`exec /init.elf`): a SIGUSR1 handler runs (`caught signal 10`) and control **returns to main** via sigreturn; `SIG_IGN` drops SIGUSR2; `kill(child, SIGTERM)` → `waitpid` status **143** (128+15). Verified (`exec /sh.elf`, monitor `sendkey ctrl-c`): typing `hello` then Ctrl-C prints `^C` and drops to a fresh prompt — the shell installs a SIGINT handler and **survives** (then `echo ok` → `ok`). Full process suite + `cowtest` **PASS**, 0 panics. Limitations (future work): delivery only on the syscall-return path, so a pure compute loop with no syscalls won't see a signal until it makes one; `do_waitpid`/`pipe_read` aren't EINTR-aware yet; one handler at a time (single saved slot), no `sigaction`/`sigprocmask`/IRQ-path delivery. |
| v5.8.12 | **`mmap`/`munmap` — anonymous demand-zero mappings.** A new `kernel/mmap.c` + **`SYS_MMAP` (19)** / **`SYS_MUNMAP` (20)** for anonymous `MAP_PRIVATE` memory, built straight on the v5.8.9 demand-paging fault handler. `do_mmap` only records a **VMA** (`{start, end, prot}` in a new `process_t.mmap_vmas[16]`) and bumps a per-process pointer — *no pages are allocated*; the region lives in **[MMAP_BASE=4 GiB, MMAP_MAX=112 TiB)**, above the 4 GiB heap cap and far below the 128 TiB stack, so it never collides. Pages materialise on first touch: `vm_handle_fault` (paging.c) gains an mmap block (right after the lazy-heap block, before the `pte_ptr` walk — a fresh mmap page has no PTE yet) that, for a USER not-present fault inside a VMA (`vma_find`), allocs a zeroed page and **sets the PTE directly** (not via `map_page_dir`, which forces WRITABLE) so prot is honored: writable only if `PROT_WRITE`, `NX` unless `PROT_EXEC`. `munmap` frees the present pages via a new `vm_free_range` (refcount-aware `free_page`, so a COW-shared page just drops a ref) and clears any fully-covered VMA. `fork` inherits the VMAs (already-faulted pages come across COW via `clone_page_directory_cow`, and the metadata lets the child demand-fault the rest); `execve` drops them (freed with the old address space). Verified (`exec /init.elf`): `mmap(12288)` → `0x100000000`, the 3 pages read back **demand-zero**, a pattern written across all of them (each faulted in on touch) reads back **intact**, then `munmap` releases the region; full process suite + signals + `cowtest` **PASS**, 0 panics. Limitations (future): anonymous `MAP_PRIVATE` only (no file-backed mmap), no `addr` hint / `MAP_FIXED`, no partial-unmap VMA splitting, no `mprotect`. |
| v5.8.13 | **Shell polish — I/O redirection (`>` `>>` `<`) + `grep`/`head`/`tail`.** The userspace shell (`user/sh.c`, now v0.4) gains file redirection: a new `parse_redir` pulls `<` / `>` / `>>` (and their attached forms `<f`/`>f`/`>>f`) out of each pipeline stage's argv, and the child `open()`s the target and `dup2()`s it onto fd 0/1 before `execve` — file redirections override the pipe wiring for the fd they touch. This needed **`dup2` to work on VFS file fds** (it was pipe-only): `SYS_DUP2` now branches — pipe ends still *duplicate* (reference-counted, `oldfd` stays valid), but a VFS handle **moves** to `newfd` and clears `oldfd`, so the shell's `dup2(fd,1); close(fd)` leaves exactly one owner and never double-closes an unrefcounted handle. Two `open()` flags back the modes: **`O_TRUNC`** (`vfs_open` empties an existing ramdisk file — frees its data, size=0) for `>`, and **`O_APPEND`** (`SYS_OPEN` seeds the ufd byte-offset to the file size) for `>>`. Three new coreutils: **`grep`** (print stdin/file lines containing a literal PATTERN), **`head`** (`-n N` first lines, default 10), **`tail`** (`-n N` last lines via a circular line buffer). No new syscalls — pure composition on `open`/`read`/`write`/`dup2`/`getdents`. Verified (`exec /init.elf`): a child redirects `echo` into `/tmp/rd.txt` with `O_CREAT|O_TRUNC`, a second appends with `O_APPEND`, and the parent reads back **23 bytes / "first line" + "second line"** (truncate + move-dup2 + append all correct). Verified (`exec /sh.elf`, `demo`): `echo … > /tmp/sh.txt` then `>> ` → `cat` shows both lines, `cat … | wc` → `2 6 37`, **`ls / | grep elf`** filters to the 15 `.elf`s (dirs dropped), **`cat welcome.txt | head -n 1`** prints the one line; background jobs + argv still work; 0 panics. (Deferred to a follow-up: `cd`/`pwd`/`export` — they need a per-process CWD + `chdir`/`getcwd` and an env store.) |
| v5.8.14 | **Shell polish part 2 — `cd`/`pwd`/`export`/`$VAR` on a per-process CWD.** Each process now carries a **working directory** (`process_t.cwd`, default `/`): new **`SYS_CHDIR` (21)** / **`SYS_GETCWD` (22)**, and a `path_resolve` canonicalizer (syscall.c) that joins a relative path onto the caller's cwd and collapses `.`, `..`, and redundant `/`. It's applied in `SYS_OPEN` and `SYS_GETDENTS`, so a program that `open()`s `"welcome.txt"` from `/home/user` hits `/home/user/welcome.txt`. `chdir` validates the target with a new `vfs_isdir` (rejects a file); the cwd is **inherited across `fork`** and kept across `execve`. On top of that the shell (`sh.c`, v0.5) gains three in-process **builtins** — `cd [dir]` (→ `chdir`, default `/home/user`), `pwd` (→ `getcwd`), `export NAME=value` — plus **`$VAR` / `$?` expansion** from a shell-local env (execve still carries no envp, so `$VAR` lives in the shell): `expand_vars` runs on every line before parsing, and `try_builtin` routes cd/pwd/export from both the REPL and the `demo`. Verified (`exec /init.elf`): `getcwd` → `/`, `chdir("/home/user")` → `/home/user`, a **relative** `open("welcome.txt")` reads 26 bytes, `chdir("..")` → `/home` (normalization). Verified (`exec /sh.elf`, `demo`): `pwd`→`/`, `cd /home/user`+`pwd`→`/home/user`, **`cat welcome.txt`** (relative — the forked child inherits the shell's cwd) prints the file, `export NAME=NyxOS` + **`echo hello $NAME`** → `hello NyxOS`; redirection/grep/pipes/bg from v5.8.13 still work; 0 panics. (The interactive shell is now genuinely usable: pipelines, redirection, job control, cwd, and variables.) |
| v5.8.15 | **Userland file tools — `mkdir`/`rm`/`touch`/`sort`/`find` (+ a real VFS bug fix).** Two new syscalls — **`SYS_MKDIR` (23)** / **`SYS_UNLINK` (24)**, both cwd-relative via `copy_path_from_user` — expose `vfs_mkdir`/`vfs_unlink` to ring 3, and five new coreutils land: `mkdir.elf`/`rm.elf`/`touch.elf` (thin wrappers; touch = `open(O_CREAT)`+close), `sort.elf` (read-all → line split → insertion sort; static buffers, since `read()`'s `copy_to_user` can't fault untouched lazy-heap pages in), and `find.elf` (recursive `getdents` walk with per-level static entry pools, optional name-substring filter). Wiring them exposed **two pre-existing VFS bugs**: (1) `resolve_parent` (vfs.c) never advanced its `result` in the walk loop, so a path ending in an existing directory at depth ≥ 2 returned the walk's *start* node instead of the true parent — `vfs_unlink("/tmp/mkd")` (and rename) on nested dirs always failed, incl. from the kernel shell; fixed by tracking `result = dir` before each descent. (2) When an intermediate component is missing, `resolve_parent` returns the deepest existing dir, so `vfs_mkdir("/no/such/parent/dir")` silently created `/no`; guarded at the syscall (`SYS_MKDIR` requires the parent to already be a directory via `vfs_isdir`), leaving kernel-shell semantics untouched. Verified (`exec /init.elf`): `mkdir=0, create+unlink=0, reopen=-1, rmdir=0, bad-mkdir=-1` — all expected. Verified (`exec /sh.elf`, `demo`): `mkdir /tmp/proj` → `touch b.txt a.txt` → **`find /tmp`** recursively lists `/tmp/proj/` + both files → **`ls /tmp/proj \| sort`** reorders `b,a → a.txt b.txt` through a pipe → `rm` both; full regression + 0 panics. (20 user ELFs now ship in the initramfs.) |
| v5.8.16 | **Raw tty + shell line editor — arrow-key history and cursor editing (+ a real keyboard-driver bug fix).** New **`SYS_TTYMODE` (25)**: a per-process stdin discipline (`process_t.tty_raw`, reset by `execve`, not inherited by `fork`). Canonical stays the v5.8.8 kernel line read; **raw** (`stdin_read_raw`, syscall.c) returns bytes as they arrive with NO echo, blocking/EINTR like the canonical path, and delivers extended keys as ANSI escapes (**ESC [ A/B/C/D** up/down/right/left, H/F home/end — emitted atomically, sourced from `getkey_poll()`'s keycode ring). The shell (v0.6) grows a real **`readline()`**: insert/delete at a cursor, ←/→/Home/End movement, and a 16-entry ↑/↓ **history** (in-progress line stashed while browsing; dedup against the last entry) — echo is fully client-side, redrawn per keystroke as `'\r'` + prompt + line + trailing-erase + backspaces to park the cursor. To render that correctly in the GUI, `terminal_capture_putchar` (terminal_win.c) now handles **`'\b'`** (destructive on the pending line) and **`'\r'`** (rebuild) — fixing kernel-echo backspace in the GUI terminal too, cosmetically broken since v5.8.8. **Bug fix found live:** arrows never arrived — `scancode_to_ascii` (keyboard.c) masked `sc &= 0x7F` *before* its `sc == 0xE0` prefix check (`0xE0 & 0x7F = 0x60`), so the E0 prefix could never match and **extended keys never reached the keycode ring from the IRQ path at all** (pre-existing; GUI arrow handling was dead too). Fixed by checking the raw byte first — arrows now work system-wide (verified: the kernel terminal's own line editing responded to ←/backspace as well). Verified (`exec /sh.elf`): `echo first`, `echo second`, then **↑↑ + Enter recalls and reruns `echo first`** → `first`; typing `echo abcd` then **←← + backspace deletes the 'b' mid-line** → Enter runs `echo acd` → `acd`; full `init.elf` regression + 0 panics. |
| v5.8.17 | **Shell tab completion — commands and paths, zero kernel changes.** The shell's `readline()` (v0.7) gains a Tab handler (`do_complete`, `user/sh.c`) built entirely on the existing `getdents` syscall. At the **command position** (line start, or right after a `|` — pipeline-aware) it completes shell builtins (`cd`/`pwd`/`export`/`exit`/`demo`) + the bare names of `/`'s `*.elf` programs; elsewhere it completes a **filesystem path** by `getdents`-ing the token's directory (`.` → cwd) and matching the base. A unique match is inserted with a trailing `' '` (or `'/'` for a directory); several candidates extend to their **longest common prefix**, or — when already ambiguous — are printed on a fresh line and the prompt is redrawn (the client-side echo model from v5.8.16 makes the reprint trivial). Candidate/dirent buffers are `.bss` statics (getdents' `copy_to_user` can't fault the one-page user stack's lazy neighbors, and 64 dirents would overflow it anyway). Verified live (`exec /sh.elf`, monitor `sendkey tab`): `ec`+Tab → `echo ` → `echo hello` prints `hello`; `t`+Tab lists `tail  touch`; `ls /ho`+Tab → `ls /home/` lists `user/`. No kernel changes, full regression + 0 panics. |
| v5.8.18 | **File-backed `mmap` + `mprotect`.** Extends the v5.8.12 anonymous VM. **File-backed mmap**: `mmap(…, fd, …)` without `MAP_ANONYMOUS` snapshots the file into a private kernel buffer at map time (`do_mmap` `kmalloc`+`vfs_pread`; the fd → VFS handle is resolved in the `SYS_MMAP` case, which has the ufd table), stored on the VMA (`vma_t.file_buf`/`file_size`). A demand-faulted page in that VMA copies its slice from the buffer (`vm_handle_fault`, after the zero-fill) and zero-fills any tail past EOF — so reading the mapping returns file contents, still one page at a time. The buffer is VMA-owned: freed on `munmap`/`execve`, **deep-copied on `fork`** (so neither side double-frees), and released at reap via a new `mmap_free_bufs` wired into all three address-space teardown sites. **`SYS_MPROTECT` (26)**: `mprotect(addr,len,prot)` rewrites the flag bits of present pages (writable iff `PROT_WRITE`, NX unless `PROT_EXEC`) via a new `vm_protect_range` (paging.c) and updates the overlapping VMAs' `prot` so pages faulted in later use it. (v1 limits: file mappings start at file offset 0 — the syscall ABI marshals only 5 args, so mmap's `offset`/a6 isn't available; a partial mprotect updates the whole overlapping VMA's prot; ramdisk files.) Verified (`exec /init.elf`): `mmap(welcome.txt, 26)` → reading the mapping yields `Type 'help' for commands.`; an anonymous `PROT_READ` page reads back zero, then `mprotect(RO→RW)` lets a write succeed (`wrote ok=1`) — a write without the mprotect would fault a read-only page and panic, so this proves the flip took. Full process suite + anonymous mmap + `cowtest` still **PASS**, 0 panics. |
| v5.8.19 | **`/dev` special files — `null`/`zero`/`random`/`urandom`, no new syscalls.** Real VFS nodes under `/dev` whose read/write are intercepted by a new `dev_type` field on `vfs_node_t`: **`/dev/null`** EOFs on read and swallows writes, **`/dev/zero`** reads endless zero bytes, **`/dev/random`** (and its `urandom` alias) yields pseudo-random bytes from a lazily-seeded xorshift64 PRNG (`get_ticks()`). `vfs_pread` special-cases `dev_type` before the size/EOF check (device content is generated, not stored in `ino->data`); `vfs_pwrite` returns the byte count and discards. Created in `vfs_init` (each via `vfs_open(O_CREAT)` then setting `dev_type`), so they're ordinary nodes to everything else — `getdents`/`ls /dev` lists them, `open`/`read`/`write` from ring 3 just work, and `>` redirection targets them. Verified (`exec /init.elf`): `/dev/zero` → 16 bytes all-zero; `/dev/null` → read=0, write=10-discarded; `/dev/random` → 8 varied bytes. Verified (`exec /sh.elf`, `demo`): `ls /dev` → `null zero random urandom`, `echo … > /dev/null` swallowed. 0 panics. |
| v5.8.20 | **6-argument syscalls — `mmap` gets a real `offset`.** The syscall entry only marshaled 5 args (the six SysV integer registers held `no` + `a1..a5`), so `mmap`'s 6th argument (`offset`) was unreachable. `syscall_entry` (isr_stubs.asm) now passes **arg6 (user R9, saved at frame+48) as the 7th C argument on the stack** — `push qword [rsp+48]` before `call syscall_handler`, `add rsp, 8` after to restore the frame base for the RAX-slot write (alignment is free to ignore: the kernel is `-mno-sse`, so no `movaps`). `syscall_handler` grew a 7th `a6` param. `SYS_MMAP` threads `a6` to `do_mmap`, which for a file mapping now snapshots the file **from `offset`** (`vfs_pread(handle, buf, size-off, off)`) so a faulted page copies `file_buf[page_offset]` and mapping byte 0 == file[offset]. Verified (`exec /init.elf`): `mmap(welcome.txt, offset 5)` → the mapping starts at `'help' for commands.` (byte 5 of `Type 'help'…`), while offset 0 still reads the whole file — and, crucially, the **entire syscall path is unchanged for the 20+ other calls**: the full process suite (fork+waitpid 123, execve 42, signals 143, dup2, pipe, mprotect, /dev, coreutils) all still **PASS**, 0 panics. This also unblocks any future 6-arg syscall. |
| v5.8.21 | **Userspace `ps` + `kill` — process management from ring 3.** A new **`SYS_GETPROCS` (27)** snapshots the process table into fixed 48-byte records `{ u32 pid, ppid, state, cpu_time; char comm[32] }` — the process analogue of `getdents` (`getprocs(buf,max)`; validated `user_ptr_ok` buffer, skips empty/pid-0 slots, interrupts masked so the table can't reshape mid-walk). Two new coreutils consume it: **`ps.elf`** prints a formatted table (`PID PPID S TIME CMD`; right-justified numeric columns since the user `printf` has no left-justify, `state`→`R`/`S`/`Z`/`P` char, buffer is a `.bss` static so `copy_to_user` never hits an unfaulted lazy-heap page), and **`kill.elf`** is an argv front-end over the existing `SYS_KILL` — `kill [-SIG] pid...` accepting a numeric (`-9`) or named (`-KILL`/`-SIGKILL`) signal, default `SIGTERM`. No kernel signal changes — `kill` just wires argv to `kill()`. Verified (`exec /init.elf`): `getprocs` → **5 processes, self visible=1** (init/idle/elf/compositor with correct states), full process suite still **PASS**, 0 panics. Verified (`exec /sh.elf`): `ps` renders the live table; `spin &` (`[bg] pid 23`) → `kill 23` → `ps` shows pid 23 **Z (zombie)** → shell reaps it `[done] pid 23 (status 143)` — **143 = 128+SIGTERM**, the canonical "killed by signal 15" encoding, proving the whole `kill.elf`→`kill()`→deliver→terminate→reap chain. (22 user ELFs now ship.) |
| v5.8.22 | **`/proc` filesystem — live kernel state as files (no new syscalls) + real process names.** A generated pseudo-filesystem under `/proc`, built on the v5.8.19 `dev_type` pattern: two new `vfs_node_t` tags (`proc_type`/`proc_pid`) mark nodes whose content is **synthesized on read** by `proc_generate` in `vfs_pread` (nothing stored in `ino->data`). Static files created at boot — **`/proc/version`** (kernel banner), **`/proc/meminfo`** (`MemTotal`/`MemUsed`/`MemFree` from `memory_total`/`memory_used`), **`/proc/uptime`** (`tick_count` at 1000 Hz → `s.cc`), **`/proc/cpuinfo`**. The **per-process `/proc/<pid>/{status,cmdline}`** dirs are reconciled with the live process table by `proc_sync()` (add live pids, drop dead ones — preempt-guarded), called at `vfs_open`/`vfs_isdir` so `ls /proc`, `cat /proc/<pid>/status`, and `cd /proc/<pid>` all see current pids — **zero new syscalls**, it's all plain `open`/`read`/`getdents`. Also fixes the v5.8.21 wart where every exec'd process showed `comm="elf"`: a new `proc_set_comm(p, path)` sets `comm` to the path basename minus `.elf` (and records `cmdline`), applied in `do_execve` (now takes the `path`), `spawn_user_path`, and the auto-init loader. Verified (`exec /init.elf`, 0 panics): `/proc/version` → `NyxOS version 5.8.22 (x86_64)`, `/proc/meminfo` → `MemTotal: 261631 kB`, `/proc/uptime` → `2.77`; `getprocs`/`ps` now show real names (`init`/`idle`/`compositor`, not `elf`); `ls /proc` lists the static files **plus** `1/ 2/ 3/ 4/ 5/` per-pid dirs; full process suite still **PASS**. |
| v5.8.23 | **Userspace `top` — a live process monitor (+ timed input + terminal clear).** The first interactive full-screen TUI. A new **`SYS_READKEY` (28)** — `readkey(timeout_ms)` — blocks up to `timeout_ms` for one key and returns it (0 on timeout, `-EINTR` on a signal), implemented via `getkey_poll` + a wrap-safe `tick_count` deadline (`sti;hlt` between polls). This is the first **timed-input** primitive: a TUI can wait a fixed interval for a command and fall through to redraw when nothing was pressed. **`top.elf`** uses it — every ~1.5 s it clears the screen (ANSI `ESC[2J`/`ESC[H`), reads `getprocs()` + the first line of `/proc/meminfo`, and redraws the `PID PPID S TIME CMD` table, staying responsive to `q`. To make the clear work, `terminal_capture_putchar` (terminal_win.c) gained a **minimal CSI escape state machine** — it recognizes `ESC[…J` (clear the scrollback) and `ESC[…H`/`f` (home) and swallows any other CSI sequence, so escapes render as a clean screen instead of literal `[2J` garbage. (A foreground process still freezes the GUI window — the compositor is parked in `kwait` — so the live refresh streams to serial and the window shows the final frame on exit.) Verified (`exec /init.elf`): `readkey(150ms)` → `0` (timeout). Verified (`exec /top.elf`, monitor `sendkey`): **4 refresh frames** at ~1.5 s (`refresh #0…#3`), each showing 5 procs with real `comm` names (`init`/`idle`/`compositor`/`top`) + `MemTotal: 261631 kB`; `sendkey q` → `top: bye`, `exit(0)` — screenshot shows a clean cleared frame. 0 panics. (23 user ELFs now ship.) |
| v5.8.24 | **Foreground jobs no longer freeze the desktop — TUIs run live in the GUI.** Before, `exec <elf>` ran the child then blocked the compositor thread in `kwait()`, so the terminal window froze until the job exited (a running `top` was only visible on serial). Now `cmd_exec` (kernel.c) does a **non-blocking foreground wait**: a loop that recomposites the desktop (`compositor_redraw_now()`, a new public wrapper over the compositor's static `redraw_all()`) and `sleep(60)`s (~16 fps) while the child runs preemptively, breaking when it becomes a `PROC_ZOMBIE` and then reaping it with `kwait()` (immediate — already a zombie). No input contention: the compositor's own event loop is suspended (nested inside this handler), so the child drains the keyboard ring itself via its `read()`/`readkey()` syscalls; and only `reap_zombies()` runs on this thread, which is busy in the loop, so the exit code is never collected out from under us. `redraw_all()` is pure draw (no printf, no input), safe to call re-entrantly from the command handler the key dispatch invoked. Verified (`exec /top.elf`): the GUI **Terminal window now refreshes live** — screenshots 3 s apart show `refresh #8` → `#15` climbing in-window (previously frozen); `q` still quits cleanly (`top: bye`, `exit(0)`) and the prompt returns. Verified (`exec /init.elf`): the full batch suite (fork, execve→42, signals→143, getprocs, readkey→0) still **PASS** through the new wait path, 0 panics. This unblocks every future full-screen TUI (editors, monitors). |
| v5.8.25 | **`edit` — a full-screen text editor (terminal cursor addressing + Ctrl keys).** The first real TUI application. Three pieces come together: **(1) Terminal cursor addressing** — `terminal_capture_putchar` (terminal_win.c) grew a full CSI parser (collects numeric params) that recognizes `ESC[<r>;<c>H` (position), `ESC[2J` (clear), `ESC[K` (clear to EOL). The first such sequence flips the terminal into a **screen mode**: `lines[]` becomes a fixed cell grid (`out_row`/`out_col` cursor) drawn from row 0 with a block cursor, instead of the scrollback+input-line model; it reverts (`terminal_capture_reset`) when the command finishes. **(2) Ctrl keys** — the keyboard IRQ now maps Ctrl+A..Z to control bytes `0x01..0x1A` (Ctrl-C still → SIGINT) so TUIs can bind commands; and `readkey(0)` now **blocks forever** (0 = no timeout) for editors. **(3) `edit.elf`** (`user/edit.c`) — loads a file into a `.bss` line buffer, then a raw-tty `readkey` loop: printable insert, Enter split, Backspace/Del join, arrows/Home/End/PgUp/PgDn, `Ctrl-O` save, `Ctrl-X` exit; each keystroke it repaints the visible slice + a status bar (`file  Ln/n  Col  ^O Save ^X Exit`) and parks the cursor. Verified live (`exec /edit.elf`, monitor `sendkey`): typed `hello nyx`⏎`line two`, the GUI showed the text with the block cursor + status bar updating (`Ln 2/2 Col 9`); `Ctrl-O` → `Saved 2 lines, 19 bytes`; `Ctrl-X` → clean exit; **`cat /noname.txt` read back exactly `hello nyx\nline two`** — the file persisted, and the terminal reverted to scrollback. init.elf regression still **PASS**, 0 panics. (24 user ELFs now ship.) |
