For `gui.c`, the serial log confirms `gui_demo` was actually running (`Launching GUI demo...`) and two four-second press-and-holds **without moving the pointer** — the exact `steps == 0` condition — produced **0 PANIC, 0 Exception**. For `fb.c`, a real logout showed a clean login screen with no trace of the session behind it, and the second desktop came up **completely clean** — no Calculator, no Minesweeper, no second Terminal from the session that had just ended, which is the stale back buffer the fix clears. Harness kept at `scratchpad/guiverify.py`. **No new syscalls** (still 52). Regression: 4-CPU smpstress **0 integrity failures**, `threads` **8000/8000 OK**, `pstorm 30` **0 faulted / 0 MAPCORRUPT / 0 PANIC**, and no faults across every GUI run. |
| v5.9.0-LTS | **P0.1 CLOSED — the intermittent ring-3 `[fault]` was a preemptible CR3-switch→iretq window in the syscall-exit path. LTS release.** The one release-blocking bug in the default configuration, open across the whole SMP arc, is root-caused and fixed with a single `cli`. **The mechanism, found by REASONING after in-guest instrumentation was proven to perturb the race away:** the syscall-exit path (`isr_stubs.asm`) does `mov cr3,[user]` and then runs ~20 instructions (CPL 0, already on the USER CR3, committed to ring 3) before the `iretq`. If a timer preempts the task *in that window*, `irq_common` saves a frame whose CS reads 0x08 — genuinely ring 0 — so `irq_scheduler_tick` resumes it on the KERNEL CR3 under its rule "a ring-0 frame switches to user itself before its iretq". But this task has ALREADY switched, so its `push 0x1B; iretq` returns to ring 3 **on the kernel CR3**; the first user instruction fetch faults with CR2==RIP. **Why it matched every observed property:** the window is only preemptible with IF=1, and syscall ENTRY masks IF via SF_MASK — so a *simple* syscall runs cli'd end-to-end and can never be hit. Only BLOCKING syscalls (`futex_wait`, `do_waitpid`, poll, pipe read) `sti` to yield and *return with IF=1* (verified: both break paths of `futex_wait` leave IF set), which is why the fault was **always in `threads`** (the only futex-heavy workload), **load-dependent** (the timer must land in ~20 instructions), and **present in the default single-core config** (it is a preemption-window bug, NOT an SMP race). It also explains why every earlier scheduler-*decision* probe showed a "correct" `saw_cs=0x8`: the decision *is* correct for a ring-0 frame; the frame cannot reveal that the CR3 switch already happened. **The fix:** one `cli` immediately after `signal_dispatch`, before the user-CR3 reload — making the CR3-switch→iretq window non-preemptible; the `iretq` restores the user RFLAGS (IF=1) atomically as it crosses to ring 3. Covers every syscall regardless of what it left IF as; the entry side needs no equivalent (IF=0 from SF_MASK) and the irq/isr return paths run under interrupt gates (IF=0), so no other such window exists. **Verified in the linked binary** (`fa` cli at 0x100343, between `call signal_dispatch` and `mov %gs:0x10,%rax`) and by an **interleaved A/B under build load** alternating the two ISOs every boot: baseline-without-cli reproduced P0.1 (`pid 7 (threads) … cs=0x1b <ON-KERNEL-CR3>`) while the cli build ran **23 valid rounds, 0 faults** under identical contention. The proof is primarily *mechanistic* — the cli closes the only (CPL0 + user-CR3 + IF=1) window in the kernel — with the A/B as the corroborating control. Also this arc: the fault diagnostic was corrected (`isr_common` now passes the faulting CR3 to `isr_handler`; a prior `read_cr3()` there reported the kernel CR3 on *every* ring-3 fault, an artefact that had driven five dead hypotheses), and `sched_forget()` closes a `sched_cur` use-after-free at both `kfree(process)` sites. **No new syscalls** (still 52). Regression on the fixed build: 4-CPU smpstress **0 integrity failures**, `threads` **8000/8000 OK**, `pstorm 30` **0 faulted / 0 MAPCORRUPT / 0 PANIC**; 23+ clean loaded rounds. **Known limits (opt-in, OFF by default, documented):** `smpbalance on` process-spreading and thread-group un-pinning still wedge under fork/exec churn (P2.x) — the default configuration does not touch them, and the VFS/terminal remain unlocked against a user process deliberately placed on an AP. | 
| v5.9.1 | **"Nightfall" desktop — a central GUI palette, and the brand purple everywhere the placeholder green used to be.** First increment of the visual track that follows the LTS. **The refactor that made it possible: `kernel/theme.h`.** Colours were `fb_rgb(r,g,b)` literals scattered across `compositor.c` and every `*_win.c`, so changing the accent meant hunting call sites and the desktop could not be themed at all. Call sites now name a ROLE — `THEME_ACCENT`, `THEME_WINDOW_BG`, `THEME_BORDER`, `THEME_ROW_DIV`, `THEME_TASKBAR_*`, `THEME_TEXT`, `THEME_ON_ACCENT` — and the values live in one header. They are macros rather than a const struct because `fb_rgb()` is a runtime function, which also leaves the door open for a future Settings→accent by swapping them for a table lookup **without touching a single call site**. **The brand fix:** the accent is the brand purple `{130,90,210}` — the wallpaper's own default "Morado" — replacing the leftover placeholder green `fb_rgb(60,120,60)` on the Start-menu header band and its title text, and driving `taskbar_hl` so the **active Menu button and the focused window** now carry the accent. The window controls keep their traffic-light scheme (close red / minimise grey / maximise green): that green is a control affordance, not branding, and recolouring it would have broken a universal convention. **One accent swap was WRONG and the screenshot caught it.** The workspace indicator's active marker was moved to the accent like everything else — but it is drawn on the DESKTOP (`fh - TASKBAR_H - 10`), not the taskbar, and the wallpaper's base colour is user-selectable. Brand purple on the default purple wallpaper rendered the ACTIVE marker nearly invisible while the inactive greys stood out — exactly backwards, and strictly worse than the green it replaced. Zooming that 14x6 px region 8x is what exposed it; at full-screen scale it looked fine. Fixed with a dedicated `THEME_INDICATOR_ON`/`_OFF` pair (near-white vs dark grey) whose comment states the rule: anything painted over the wallpaper must be light-vs-dark, never accent-coloured, because it has to survive all eleven wallpaper choices. The About window's "NyxOS Desktop" heading also moved green → accent (it sits on a dark panel, so the accent reads fine there). **The desktop itself:** `draw_background()` rewritten as a Nightfall sky — a purple vertical gradient, a glowing moon built from concentric halos plus craters, and **110 deterministic LCG-seeded stars** that avoid the moon's area so the composition stays readable at any resolution. Two small helpers back it: `bg_isqrt()` (integer Newton square root — no FPU in the kernel) and `bg_fill_circle()` (horizontal-span fill). Determinism matters here: the star field is seeded, so every boot paints the identical sky rather than flickering to a new one on each repaint. **Branding swept beyond the GUI:** the README badges were still shields-green `00ff9d` while the brand is purple — all seven are now `825AD2`, so the repo, the login screen and the desktop finally agree on one colour. **No new syscalls** (still 52). No kernel-internals change: this increment is compositor + docs only, so the v5.9.0-LTS guarantees (P0.1 closed, 23 clean loaded rounds) carry over untouched. |
| v5.9.111 | **Maintenance: correctness read of the `net/tcp.c` state machine — fixed a peer's final data+FIN segment being half-handled, so a graceful close stalled until the peer retransmitted a bare FIN.** Read the whole path: connection lookup (`find_conn_by_tuple` matches the 4-tuple and skips listeners; `find_listener` matches by port only), the pseudo-header checksum (network-order, matches the IP header on the wire), `send_segment` (arms retransmit only for sequence-consuming segments — SYN/FIN/data — and advances `seq` correctly for each), `tcp_tick` (RTO with exponential backoff and signed-wraparound deadline compare), the passive-open child-spawn on a SYN to a listener, and the no-conn RST path (which correctly never RST-answers an RST, avoiding a loopback storm). The cumulative-ACK retransmit-clear and the v5.9.104 recv-OOM drop are intact. **The real bug was the receive dispatch (`tcp_handle_packet`): data, SYN-ACK and FIN were an `if / else if / else if` chain**, so a segment carrying **both** the peer's last data **and** its FIN (extremely common — a server piggybacks FIN on its final data segment, e.g. an HTTP/1.0 response that closes) took only the data branch: the payload was stored but the **FIN was ignored** — we stayed `ESTABLISHED`, never acked the FIN (so `tcp_recv_ready` never signalled EOF), and only recovered once the peer's RTO fired and it retransmitted a standalone FIN. **Fixed** by pulling FIN handling out of the `else if` into its own check that runs **after** the data path, acking `seq + payload_len + 1` (the FIN sits just past any data). A `data_accepted` flag guards it: if the segment carried data we had to drop under memory pressure (the recv-OOM path), the FIN is skipped too, so we never ack past bytes we didn't keep — the peer resends the whole segment. **Behaviour-preserving on every working path**: a pure-data segment, a pure FIN, and the SYN/SYN-ACK handshake are byte-for-byte unchanged; only the previously-mishandled data+FIN case now transitions to `CLOSE_WAIT` and acks the FIN immediately. **Verified live, 0 real faults** (checked `#PF`/`PANIC`/`SLABCORRUPT`/`MAPCORRUPT`, none): `tcploop` (in-guest loopback) completes the 3-way handshake, bidirectional data and graceful teardown → PASS; `tcploop 1` drops a data segment and the RTO timer retransmits and recovers → PASS; a real `httpget http://10.0.2.2:8000/` against a Linux (slirp) server returns `200 OK` with the full body and closes cleanly; and the KAT suite is unaffected — `jpegtest` 3/3, `rsatest` 7/7, `pngtest` 5/5, `bmptest` 4/4, `giftest` 7/7, `formtest` 14/14, clean boot. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1661.9 KB. Files: `net/tcp.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** in-order sequence validation in `tcp_handle_packet` (it currently appends payload without checking `seq == conn->ack`, so a duplicate or out-of-order segment mis-stores data); or `-Wsign-conversion` on `net/dns.c` + `net/dhcp.c` (untrusted packet parsing); or a correctness read of `mm/mmap.c` (VMA overlaps / partial unmap). |
| v5.9.110 | **Maintenance: correctness read of the kernel allocator (`kmalloc`/slab/heap) — fixed a heap-fragmentation flaw where adjacent free blocks were never merged, so a large allocation could fail with plenty of free memory.** Read the whole path: `kmalloc`/`kfree`/`krealloc` (memory.c) route by a per-allocation magic header (slab vs heap) and lock with `spin_lock_irqsave`; the slab allocator (slab.c) carves pages into fixed size-classes and even has a free-list corruption check in `slab_alloc` that panics if a slot's `next` points outside its page. The real weakness was in the **heap** (heap.c), a first-fit free-list: **`heap_free` coalesces only FORWARD and only ONCE** (`if (block->next && !block->next->used)`), so two physically-adjacent blocks freed while a block between them was still in use stay split forever — repeated over a long uptime of mixed alloc/free this fragments the 16 MB heap until a large request (e.g. a 64 KB HTTP response or an image plane) fails despite ample total free space. Since the free list is address-ordered and every block is physically contiguous with its list-successor (guaranteed by how `heap_alloc` splits), **`heap_alloc` now coalesces each free block with all following free blocks as it walks** — space freed in ANY order is reclaimed on the next allocation, not just the lucky free-a-block-whose-next-is-already-free case. It's the same merge `heap_free` already does, looped safely in the search. **Verified live, 0 real faults** (checked for `#PF`/`PANIC`/`SLABCORRUPT`/`MAPCORRUPT`, none): the full self-test suite — which drives hundreds of `kmalloc`/`kfree` cycles through the heap for decode buffers, bignums and render scratch — passes across repeated iterations: `jpegtest` 3/3, `rsatest` 7/7, `pngtest` 5/5, `bmptest` 4/4, `giftest` 7/7, `formtest` 14/14, plus a clean boot (the whole compositor/VFS/net init runs on this allocator). **Behaviour-preserving** — the coalesce is provably valid under the address-ordered, physically-contiguous invariant, and a run with no adjacent free blocks is a no-op — so every allocation that worked before still returns the same-shaped block, only fragmentation is reduced. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1661.8 KB (unchanged). Files: `mm/heap.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** the `net/tcp.c` state machine (transitions / seq-ACK / teardown); or `-Wsign-conversion` on `net/dns.c` + `net/dhcp.c` (untrusted packet parsing). |
| v5.9.109 | **Maintenance: correctness read of `fs/vfs.c` — fixed a `rename()` that applied HALF of a failed cross-directory move.** Read the node pool, path walker and the file operations: the VFS is largely robust — `alloc/free/release_node` guard against double-free (`on_free_list`) and recycling live handles (open-fd orphaning), `vfs_append_child` bounds every `children[]` insert, `path_token`/`resolve_path`/`resolve_parent` all copy into fixed buffers with `strncpy` caps, `vfs_pwrite` checks `offset+count` overflow, `proc_generate` always returns a non-negative `strlen` (so `vfs_pread`'s `/proc` path can't underflow), and `vfs_cp` null-checks every allocation and frees on every failure path. **The one real bug was in `vfs_rename`:** it wrote the new name into the node **before** attempting the cross-directory move, so when the destination directory was full (`vfs_append_child` hits `MAX_CHILDREN`=128 and the move aborts) the source was left **renamed but not moved** — `rename("/a/x","/b/y")` turned `/a/x` into `/a/y` instead of leaving it untouched. A failed rename must be a no-op on the source. Fixed by moving the `strncpy(ino->name, new_name)` to **after** the move has committed: the dest-full path now `return`s with the source's name *and* location unchanged, while a same-directory rename and a successful cross-directory move both still apply the new name exactly as before. **Verified live, 0 real faults** (the check filters `unhandled`/`PANIC`/`#PF`, not the word "fault"): `touch foo` → `ls` shows `foo`, `mv foo bar` → `ls` shows `bar` and `foo` is gone — the normal rename path is intact; `formtest` 14/14 and a clean boot confirm the VFS is otherwise unaffected. **Behaviour-neutral on every succeeding rename**; only the (rare) dest-full failure changes, from a half-applied rename to a clean no-op. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1661.8 KB (unchanged). Files: `fs/vfs.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** a correctness read of the slab allocator (`mm/`), or of `net/tcp.c`'s state machine; or `-Wsign-conversion` on `net/dns.c`/`dhcp.c` (untrusted packet parsing). |
| v5.9.108 | **Maintenance: `-Wsign-conversion` review of the network path (`net/http.c` + `net/tcp.c`) — fixed a latent out-of-bounds stack read in the HTTP request builder and made `http.c` sign-conversion-clean.** The HTTP client parses attacker-controlled response bytes and builds requests from attacker-influenceable URLs (a redirect `Location`, a page's link `href`), so it's a high-value target. `net/tcp.c` came back **0 hits**; `net/http.c` had **5**. Four are benign given their guards — the two Content-Length parses (`*cl` is a digit → `0..9`), the status-text `memcpy` (`st_len` is clamped to `[1,63]` first), and `total += n` (inside `if (n > 0)`) — and were made explicit with unsigned casts. **The fifth was a real latent bug:** `http_request` builds the request with `req_len = snprintf(req, sizeof(req), …)` into a **512-byte stack buffer**, and C99 `snprintf` returns the length it *would* have written — so if a long host/path overran `req[]`, `req_len` could **exceed 512**, and `tcp_send(conn, req, req_len)` would then **read past the stack buffer**, streaming kernel stack memory to the server (an info leak) or faulting. Today it fits by only ~8 bytes of margin (path ≤255 + host ≤127 + template < 512), so it's not currently reachable — but it's a fragile size-coincidence one URL-parser tweak away from exploitable, and the host/path can come from a **malicious redirect Location**. Fixed by clamping: `if (req_len < 0) return -1; if (req_len >= (int)sizeof(req)) req_len = sizeof(req)-1;` before the send, so `tcp_send` can never read beyond `req[]` (a truncated request is malformed and the server rejects it — but no OOB). With `req_len` now provably in `[0,511]`, the send's conversion is clean too, so **`http.c` compiles 0-warning under `-Wsign-conversion`** (standalone `gcc` check). `-Wsign-conversion` stays out of the global build (~1,900 tree-wide hits). **Verified live, 0 real faults:** a **real HTTP fetch** — Selene (temp default URL → a local server) downloads and renders a page end-to-end, exercising `http_request` (with the clamp), `http_parse_response` and the receive loop, "200 OK" (screenshot); plus `formtest` **14/14** (its case 10 feeds a canned `301 … Location:` straight through `http_parse_response`, hitting the touched status-text `memcpy` + Content-Length paths), and `jpegtest` 3/3 / `pngtest` 5/5 / `bmptest` 4/4 / `giftest` 7/7 / `rsatest` 7/7, clean boot. **Behaviour-neutral on the normal path** (short requests fit, so the clamp is a no-op). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1661.7 -> 1661.8 KB. Files: `net/http.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** a correctness read of `fs/vfs.c` or the slab allocator; or `-Wsign-conversion` on `net/dns.c` (2) / `net/dhcp.c` (3), which also parse untrusted packets. |
| v5.9.107 | **Maintenance: `-Wsign-conversion` audit of the untrusted image decoders — all five have sound bounds handling; the GIF decoder is now sign-conversion-clean.** Selene fetches images from arbitrary web servers, so the decoders parse fully attacker-controlled bytes — the right place to keep hunting sign bugs after `der.c`. Compiled each under `-Wsign-conversion`: **`png.c` / `bmp.c` / `inflate.c` are already 0-hit**, `gif.c` has **4**, `jpeg.c` **31**. Read the flagged paths plus the surrounding bounds logic in gif.c and jpeg.c, and both are robust against a malicious image: **gif.c** caps the frame dimensions (`fw*fh > GIF_MAX_PX` as a 64-bit product), keeps the LZW copy within the measured sub-block total (`o <= total`), and allocates planes with 64-bit multiplies; its 4 hits are all `p += ct_n*3` / an index where `ct_n = 2<<(0..7)` is provably positive — benign. **jpeg.c** caps `W`/`H` at `JPG_MAX_DIM` and `W*H` at `JPG_MAX_PX`, bounds every DQT/DHT/SOF/SOS segment against `seglen`, keeps the Huffman index `< total <= 256`, and sizes each component plane with a 64-bit `(size_t)cw*ch`; its 31 hits are the fixed-point IDCT / YCbCr / fancy-upsample arithmetic on provably-positive intermediates — benign. **Concrete change: made `gif.c` `-Wsign-conversion`-clean** (cast the four positive `gct_n`/`lct_n` products, and made the NETSCAPE-loop index unsigned) so the untrusted GIF path builds cleanly under the flag, mirroring what v5.9.106 did for `der.c`. `jpeg.c`'s 31 are left as-is (casting benign IDCT math would be pure noise) but documented as benign. `-Wsign-conversion` is NOT added globally (~1,900 tree-wide hits). **Verified live, 0 real faults:** `giftest` **7/7** (basic / transparency / interlaced / dict-growth / animated / loop-count — exercises the touched color-table + NETSCAPE paths), `jpegtest` 3/3, `pngtest` 5/5, `bmptest` 4/4, `rsatest` 7/7, `formtest` 14/14, clean boot. **Behaviour-neutral** (casts of provably-positive values). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1661.7 KB (unchanged). Files: `image/gif.c`. **Audit takeaway:** the whole image-decode surface (inflate/PNG/BMP/GIF/JPEG) that a web page can reach is bounds-sound. **Next maintenance targets:** a correctness read of `fs/vfs.c` or the slab allocator; or `-Wsign-conversion` on `net/http.c` / `net/tcp.c` (untrusted response parsing). |
| v5.9.106 | **Maintenance: `-Wsign-conversion` review of the untrusted DER/ASN.1 parser (`crypto/der.c`) — hardened the X.509 date parser, which accepted a `'Z'` in any date position, and made the file sign-conversion-clean.** `der.c` reads attacker-controlled certificate bytes during TLS, so it's the right place for a sign-bug hunt. Compiled it under `-Wsign-conversion`: **8 hits, all in one function, `parse_time`** (the UTCTime/GeneralizedTime → packed-decimal date parser). Audited the length primitives first — `der_read`/`der_enter`/`der_skip` are correctly bounds-checked (long-form length rejects indefinite encoding and >4 length bytes, and every advance re-checks the remaining buffer), so there's no OOB there. The 8 conversions themselves are benign *given* the digit guard — but that guard was **too loose**: `for (i<14) if (p[i] not digit) { if (p[i] != 'Z') return 0; }` accepted a `'Z'` in **any** of the first 14 bytes, not just as the trailing timezone marker. A malformed Time with `'Z'` in a real date slot (e.g. the month) therefore passed validation and parsed to a **bogus-but-bounded** date (`'Z'-'0'` = 42) instead of being rejected — a correctness hole in an untrusted parser that feeds the (strict-mode-enforced) validity check. **Rewrote `parse_time`:** it now requires the exact digit count for the tag (**12** for UTCTime, **14** for GeneralizedTime) plus the trailing marker byte, and rejects the value unless **every date position is an actual `0-9` digit**; a small `d2()` helper reads each two-digit field in **`unsigned`** arithmetic, so nothing converts a possibly-negative `int` to `uint64_t` — **`der.c` now compiles 0-warning under `-Wsign-conversion`** (verified by a standalone `gcc -Wsign-conversion -fsyntax-only crypto/der.c`). `-Wsign-conversion` is NOT added to the global build (the whole tree has ~1,900 mostly-benign hits); this just leaves the security-critical parser ready for it. **Verified live, 0 real faults:** `dertest` **3/3** (TLV + nesting, long-form length, EC-point extraction) and **`chaintest`/x509 5/5** — the chain self-test's **validity-date-parse case runs `parse_time` on example.com's real certificate and still passes**, proving the stricter parser accepts well-formed dates; `jpegtest` 3/3 + `formtest` 14/14 + clean boot. **Behaviour-neutral for valid certs** (real dates are all digits); a malformed Time is now cleanly rejected (return 0) instead of yielding a garbage date. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1661.7 KB (unchanged). Files: `crypto/der.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** `-Wsign-conversion` on another untrusted parser (`image/jpeg.c` or `gif.c` decode length handling); or a correctness read of `fs/vfs.c` / the slab allocator. |
| v5.9.105 | **Maintenance: a correctness read of `mm/paging.c` — the virtual→physical translator returned a WRONG address for NX pages (the NX bit leaked into the result); fixed, and the three duplicate page-frame masks collapsed to one.** Read the whole page-fault / COW / demand / mmap / teardown machinery: it is genuinely robust (every `alloc_page` is null-checked, COW privatises with refcounts, `clone_page_directory_cow` builds the child its OWN intermediate tables so teardown never double-frees shared tables, `pt_next` panics with diagnostics on a corrupted entry, TLB shootdowns are disciplined). One real latent bug surfaced: **`get_phys_addr` (and `unmap_page`) extracted frame addresses with `& ~0xFFF`**, which clears only the low 12 bits and **keeps bit 63 (NX) and any reserved bits 63:52**. So `get_phys_addr` on an **NX page** (every data page) returned `physaddr | (1<<63)` — a non-canonical garbage pointer; the huge-page and intermediate-table extractions had the same leak. It isn't triggered today only because the sole two callers (the `cowtest` self-test freeing its demand + COW pages) happen to use non-NX pages — but it is a booby-trap for the next caller. Fixed by masking with the page-frame bits **[51:12]** everywhere (`pte & PHYS_ADDR_MASK`), which drops NX/reserved and yields the true physical address. While there, the **three identical `0x000FFFFFFFFFF000` masks** (`PT_ADDR_BITS`, `PT_PHYS_MASK`, `PTE_ADDR_MASK`, defined at three different points) were consolidated to alias one canonical **`PHYS_ADDR_MASK`** — one source of truth. **Verified live, 0 real faults** (the QEMU log's four "fault" mentions are `cowtest`'s own "demand faults taken: 1" / "cow faults taken: 1" prose, not exceptions — the real unhandled/panic/#PF count is 0): **`cowtest` PASSES** — its demand page zero-fills, its COW page privatises on write while the original stays `0xAAAAAAAA` unchanged, and it then **frees both pages by the address `get_phys_addr` returned** without corrupting the allocator (proven by `jpegtest` 3/3 and `formtest` 14/14 running cleanly afterwards — a wrong physical address would have freed the wrong frame and poisoned later allocations). Clean boot, clean `make` (0 warnings). **Behaviour-neutral for the current callers** (their pages are non-NX, so the masks agree); the fix matters the moment `get_phys_addr` is used on a normal NX page. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1661.7 KB (unchanged). Files: `mm/paging.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** a targeted `-Wsign-conversion` review of one crypto file; or a correctness read of another subsystem (vfs, or the slab allocator). |
| v5.9.104 | **Maintenance: error-path audit — found and fixed a remotely-triggerable NULL-deref kernel crash in the TCP receive path (unchecked `kmalloc` under memory pressure).** Audited all **81 `kmalloc` call sites** in the kernel (a heuristic flagged the ones whose result isn't null-checked within a few lines; each candidate was then read by hand). **79 were already safe** — most flagged sites were false positives (a struct-field assignment like `n->data = kmalloc(...)` checked as `if (!n->data)`, or a run of allocations sharing one combined `if (!a || !b || …) { free each; }` cleanup, e.g. Selene's 8-buffer context and `render_html`'s 5 buffers — all correct). **Two were real bugs, both in `net/tcp.c`'s receive-data path** (`tcp_receive`): the initial `conn->recv_buf = kmalloc(...)` and the grow-path `new_buf = kmalloc(...)` were used **without a null check**, so a peer sending data while the kernel heap was exhausted would `memcpy` through a NULL pointer and **crash the whole kernel** (a remote denial-of-service under memory pressure). Fixed by checking both allocations and, on failure, **dropping the segment cleanly** — the receive buffer and its data are left intact, `recv_len`/`ack` are **not** advanced and no ACK is sent, so the peer simply retransmits later (standard TCP flow control); `conn->ack` is now assigned only *after* the data is known to be storable (was set up-front). **Verified live, 0 faults:** with Selene's default URL temporarily pointed at a local server, a **14.8 KB page** (well over the initial 4096-byte recv buffer, so the reallocation/grow path runs several times) is fetched and rendered in full — "TCP RECV GROW-PATH TEST" heading + paragraphs, "200 OK" — proving the restructured path is byte-for-byte correct on the success side; the KAT suite (`jpegtest` 3/3, `rsatest` 7/7, `pngtest` 5/5, `bmptest` 4/4, `giftest` 7/7, `formtest` 14/14) and a clean boot all pass. **Behaviour-neutral on the normal path** (only the OOM edge changes, from crash to graceful drop); clean `make` still 0 warnings; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1661.7 KB (unchanged). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**. Files: `net/tcp.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** a targeted `-Wsign-conversion` review of one crypto file; or a subsystem-by-subsystem correctness read (mm/paging next, since net just got a pass). |
| v5.9.103 | **Maintenance: `-Wshadow` sweep — fixed the two variable-shadowing warnings and made `-Wshadow` a permanent build flag, so the kernel can never silently re-introduce a shadow bug.** A probe build with `-Wshadow -Wconversion` added measured the volume first: **`-Wshadow` = 2**, but **`-Wconversion` + `-Wsign-conversion` ≈ 1,900** — the latter almost all deliberate, safe narrowings (`uint8_t x = expr`, size↔int) typical of systems code, where a blanket "fix" would be a huge, noisy, near-zero-value diff. So this increment takes the high-signal, fully-closable half: **`-Wshadow`.** Both hits were real shadows (an inner local hiding an outer one — a classic source of "edited the wrong variable" bugs): (1) `net/dhcp.c` — inside the reply loop, `uint32_t cookie` (the magic cookie read **from** the RX packet) shadowed the outer `cookie` (the one written **into** the DISCOVER/REQUEST packet); the inner is renamed **`rx_cookie`** (matching the nearby `rx_xid`), so the two distinct values are now distinct names. (2) `gui/core/compositor.c` — the right-click desktop handler re-declared `uint32_t fh = fb_get_height()`, shadowing the function-level `fh` from `compositor_run`; since the framebuffer height is fixed, the redundant inner read is simply removed and the outer `fh` used. With both fixed, **`-Wshadow` is added to `CFLAGS` for good** (`makefile`), turning a one-time cleanup into an enforced invariant. **`-Wconversion` was deliberately NOT pursued** — its ~1,900 hits are noise; a future increment could instead run `-Wsign-conversion` over a single security-sensitive file (e.g. one crypto module) where a sign bug would actually matter. **Verified:** a clean `make` (now with `-Wshadow` on) emits **0 warnings / 0 errors**; **0 faults** on a full boot with `jpegtest` 3/3, `rsatest` 7/7, `pngtest` 5/5, `bmptest` 4/4, `giftest` 7/7, `formtest` 14/14, and — since `dhcp.c` was touched — **DHCP still leases** (`OFFER`/`ACK` for 10.0.2.15, which requires the renamed `rx_cookie == DHCP_MAGIC_COOKIE` check to pass), confirming the rename is behaviour-identical. **Behaviour-neutral by construction** (a rename + a redundant-read removal); `nyx-kernel.bin` 1661.7 KB (unchanged). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**. Files: `makefile`, `net/dhcp.c`, `gui/core/compositor.c`. **Next maintenance targets:** a targeted `-Wsign-conversion` review of one crypto file; error-path auditing (every `kmalloc`/fetch/parse checks failure + cleans up); or a subsystem-by-subsystem correctness read (net / mm-paging). |
| v5.9.102 | **Maintenance: dead-code + stale-comment sweep — removed two never-called functions and corrected three comments that contradicted shipped features.** A `TODO`/`FIXME`/`XXX`/`HACK` scan of the kernel (excluding the third-party `doom_src/`) came back **empty** — no explicit debt markers — and the build has no unused-static-function warnings, so the dead code that remained was the kind `-Wall` can't see: two functions explicitly silenced with **`__attribute__((unused))`** (kept "just in case", never wired up). Both are removed after confirming zero callers: **`cpuid_x2apic_detected`** (`drivers/misc/apic.c` — an x2APIC CPUID probe; NyxOS runs xAPIC) and **`taskbar_clock_hit`** (`gui/core/compositor.c` — a taskbar-clock click hit-test that was never hooked up; the clock is display-only). Then a scan for comments asserting the absence of now-shipped features found **three stale ones**, all corrected: `gui/apps/selene_win.c` said *"NyxOS has no image decoder yet, so we show the alt text in a box"* (false since the v5.9.82–90 PNG/BMP/GIF/JPEG arc — images are decoded and drawn, the box is only the fallback) and *"HTTP only - no TLS yet"*, and `gui/apps/selene_win.h` said *"HTTP only for now (no TLS)"* (both false since v5.9.56 — Selene fetches `https://` over TLS 1.2). A companion check confirmed the other `Generated`-marked headers are legit (KAT fixtures / a static DOOM config) and the remaining "for now"/"placeholder" comments are accurate scope notes, not rot. **Behaviour-neutral by construction:** the removed functions had no callers (and were already `--gc-sections`'d out of the binary, so `nyx-kernel.bin` is unchanged at 1661.7 KB), and comment edits change no code. **Verified:** clean `make` still **0 warnings / 0 errors**; **0 faults** on a full boot with `jpegtest` 3/3, `rsatest` 7/7, `pngtest` 5/5, `bmptest` 4/4, `giftest` 7/7, `formtest` 14/14 all passing (apic.c + compositor.c both run during boot, so a clean boot exercises them). Also **confirmed the v5.9.101 fix in practice: after this build, `git status` does NOT list `kernel/fs/initramfs_data.h`** — the churn is gone for good. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**. Files: `drivers/misc/apic.c`, `gui/core/compositor.c`, `gui/apps/selene_win.c`, `gui/apps/selene_win.h`. **Next maintenance targets:** a `-Wshadow`/`-Wconversion` deeper warning pass (will surface real sign/precision issues), or a subsystem-by-subsystem correctness review (net, mm/paging, or crypto). |
| v5.9.101 | **Maintenance: the generated `initramfs_data.h` blob is no longer tracked in git — clean builds stop churning 27k lines — and the last build warning (the NASM one, deferred at v5.9.100) is now fixed, so `make` is 100% warning-clean.** Root cause of the v5.9.100 finding, run down: `tools/mkinitramfs.py` is fully deterministic (cpio headers zero mtime/uid/gid/ino, files added in `sorted()` order), and same-source builds ARE byte-identical — so the churn wasn't non-determinism. The real issue was a **tracking inconsistency**: the userspace `*.elf`/`*.so` that the blob embeds are (correctly) git-ignored build artifacts, but `initramfs_data.h` — a *second-order* artifact regenerated from them by the Makefile's `FORCE` rule on **every** build — was still tracked, and the current toolchain rebuilds it byte-differently from the committed copy, so each clean build showed a ~27k-line diff. Fix: **`git rm --cached kernel/fs/initramfs_data.h` + a `.gitignore` rule** (the `.tmp` sibling was already covered by `*.tmp`). It's a pure build artifact now — a clean checkout regenerates it via `make` (which already ran mkinitramfs every build), so nothing in git needs it. A scan of the other tracked `Generated`-marked headers confirmed they're **generate-once fixtures** (`image/*_vectors.h` KAT data, `doom_src/config.h`) that the build never rewrites — only `initramfs_data.h` was regenerated per-build, so it was the sole offender. **With the blob untracked, the v5.9.100-deferred crt0 warning is now safe to fix:** `user/crt0.asm:21` `mov [environ], rdx` → `mov [abs environ], rdx` — an explicit-absolute operand that silences NASM's `implicit DEFAULT ABS is deprecated` **without changing the encoding** (user binaries are fixed-VA / non-PIE, so the operand was already absolute), and any resulting blob regeneration is now ignored rather than committed. **Verified:** a clean `make` now emits **0 warnings / 0 errors — the whole build (C *and* NASM) is warning-clean for the first time**; after a build, `git status` shows `initramfs_data.h` only as the one-time staged removal (`D`), never again as a modification. Regressions + userspace both intact, **0 faults**: `jpegtest` 3/3, `formtest` 14/14, and **`init.elf` (a userspace ELF that boots through `crt0`) loads + registers PID 6 and `ps` runs** — confirming the `[abs]` change didn't disturb userspace startup. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1661.7 KB (unchanged). Files: `.gitignore`, `user/crt0.asm`, `kernel/core/kernel.h` (+ `kernel/fs/initramfs_data.h` **removed from tracking** — commit the `D`, and if a rebuild regenerates the on-disk file it's now ignored). **Next maintenance targets:** a TODO/FIXME + dead-code sweep, a `-Wshadow`/`-Wconversion` deeper warning pass, or a subsystem-by-subsystem correctness review. |
| v5.9.100 | **START OF THE GENERAL-MAINTENANCE PASS — the kernel's C compile now emits ZERO warnings (14 of the 15 build warnings fixed), and a real undefined-behaviour bug + two latent hazards were fixed along the way.** Per the standing plan, v5.9.100 stops feature work and begins a correctness/cleanup sweep; this first increment is a **compiler-warning audit**. The build already used `-Wall -Wextra`, but 15 warnings had accumulated unnoticed. Three were genuine issues, not cosmetics: (1) **`image/jpeg.c` — undefined behaviour** (`-Wshift-negative-value`): the JPEG EXTEND helper computed `v + (-1 << s) + 1`, and **left-shifting a negative value is UB in C**; rewritten as the numerically-identical `v - (1 << s) + 1` (no negative shift). `jpegtest` still **3/3** — the decode is bit-identical, proving the transform was preserved. (2) **`gui/games/games_win.h` — a macro-name collision**: the include-guard `GAMES_WIN_H` was **also** `#define`d as the window-height constant on line 18, so the guard was silently redefined on every include; the guard is renamed `GAMES_WIN_H_INCLUDED` (a scan of every `*_win.h` confirmed it was the only one). (3) **`mm/paging.c` — a dead null-check** (`-Waddress`): the stack-overflow reporter tested `p->comm ? p->comm : "?"`, but `comm` is a `char[32]` whose address is never NULL, so the fallback was dead code; simplified to `p->comm`. The other 11 were **`-Wmisleading-indentation`** (multi-statement lines whose trailing statement looked guarded but wasn't) across `crypto/rsa.c` (4), `gui/apps/selene_win.c` (5), `image/png.c` (1) and the `kmalloc`-cleanup line — all reformatted to one statement per line (no logic change). **The 15th warning is DEFERRED, not fixed:** a NASM `implicit DEFAULT ABS is deprecated` notice in `user/crt0.asm`. Fixing it (`DEFAULT ABS` / `[abs]`) is a one-liner, BUT it touches userspace, which forces a regeneration of the committed `fs/initramfs_data.h` blob — and that blob turns out to be **non-reproducible with the current toolchain** (even the *unmodified* HEAD source rebuilds it with ~13.5k differing lines, a real byte-content difference, not line-endings). Rather than bury a real 27k-line generated-blob churn inside a warning sweep, the crt0 notice is bundled with a **dedicated future increment: make the userspace/initramfs build reproducible (or stop committing the generated blob), then fix the crt0 notice + commit the regenerated blob deliberately.** **Verified:** a clean `make` now emits **0 C warnings / 0 errors** (only the one deferred NASM notice remains), and every self-test of a touched subsystem still passes — **`jpegtest` 3/3, `rsatest` 7/7, `pngtest` 5/5, `bmptest` 4/4, `giftest` 7/7, `formtest` 14/14** — plus a **clean GUI boot, 0 faults** (screenshot: desktop + windows intact after touching the `#PF` handler). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**. Files touched: `image/jpeg.c`, `image/png.c`, `crypto/rsa.c`, `gui/apps/selene_win.c`, `gui/games/games_win.h`, `mm/paging.c` (**not** `user/crt0.asm` or `fs/initramfs_data.h` — deliberately excluded, see above); `nyx-kernel.bin` 1661.7 KB (unchanged — the fixes are semantically identical). **Next maintenance targets:** the initramfs-reproducibility + crt0 notice bundle above; a TODO/FIXME + dead-code sweep; a `-Wshadow`/`-Wconversion` deeper pass; or a subsystem-by-subsystem correctness review. |
| v5.9.99 | **Selene formats `<pre>` and `<blockquote>` — code blocks keep their whitespace and quotes are indented on every line.** The last render polish before the v5.9.100 maintenance pass. Two block elements that used to collapse like any prose now read correctly (`gui/apps/selene_win.c`). **`<pre>`:** a `pre_mode` flag makes the strip **preserve whitespace literally** inside the block — interior runs of spaces are kept (not collapsed to one), a `\n` becomes a real line break, and a tab expands to `SEL_PRE_TAB` (4) spaces — so source code and ASCII art keep their layout; a single newline right after the opening `<pre>` is swallowed (browser behaviour) so the block doesn't start with a blank line, and `&lt;`/`&amp;`/… still decode inside it. **`<blockquote>`:** a new per-char **`tindent[]`** buffer records the quote-nesting level in effect at each character; `wrap_text` reads it and prefixes **every line the content lands on — hard newline *or* soft word-wrap — with `level×SEL_QUOTE_INDENT` (4) spaces**, so a quoted paragraph stays indented even where it wraps, and nested `<blockquote>`s indent further. Level 0 (all non-quoted text) is byte-for-byte unchanged. **Bug found + fixed via the KAT:** the tag-name scanner used a `name[10]` buffer capped at 9 chars, so **`blockquote` (10 letters) was truncated to `blockquot`** and never matched (it had silently been a no-op tag all along) — the buffer is now `name[16]` (also lets `figcaption` through). **Pinned — `formtest` now 14/14:** a new case asserts `<pre>a  b\n  c</pre>` renders the exact lines **`a  b`** (interior double space kept) and **`  c`** (leading indent kept), and `<blockquote>Quoted text here</blockquote>` renders **`    Quoted text here`** (4-space margin). `pngtest` 5/5 / `bmptest` 4/4 / `giftest` 7/7 / `jpegtest` 3/3 all still pass — the `<table>`, list, and wrap KATs confirm the shared `wrap_text` change didn't regress anything. **Verified live in the GUI, 0 faults** (screenshot): a C `main()` in a `<pre>` keeps its 4- and 8-space indentation, the aligned trailing `// comment`, and the decoded `<`; a `<blockquote>` wraps across three lines **all four-space-indented, including the two wrapped continuation lines**. **Honest scope:** `<pre>` tabs are a flat 4 spaces (not column tabstops) and very long pre lines still soft-wrap (no horizontal scroll); a table/list *inside* a blockquote isn't margin-shifted (only flowing text + links carry the indent). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files** (`gui/apps/selene_win.c`); `nyx-kernel.bin` 1660.4 -> 1661.7 KB. **Milestone: this is the last feature increment — v5.9.100 next begins the GENERAL MAINTENANCE pass** (features stop; a correctness/cleanup/consistency sweep over the whole tree starts). Selene now renders headings, paragraphs, rules, links, forms (GET+POST), images (PNG/BMP/GIF-animated/JPEG), tables (colspan/rowspan/nested), ordered+nested lists, ~30 entities, redirects, tabs, find-in-page, `<pre>` and `<blockquote>` — a genuinely capable in-kernel browser. |
| v5.9.98 | **Selene renders ordered + nested lists — `<ol>` items are numbered `1. 2. 3.` and a list nested in an item is indented per level.** Before, every `<li>` emitted a flat `- ` bullet regardless of `<ol>` vs `<ul>` or nesting depth, so numbered and multi-level lists read wrong. Now `render_html` (`gui/apps/selene_win.c`) keeps a small **list-context stack** (`liststk[SEL_LIST_MAXDEPTH=8]`, `{ordered, counter}` per level): `<ul>`/`<ol>` **push** (recording ordered-ness, counter=0) on open and **pop** on close; each `<li>` indents by `(depth-1)×SEL_LIST_INDENT` (2) spaces, then emits **`N. `** (incrementing that level's counter) for an ordered list or **`- `** for unordered. A list nested inside an `<li>` therefore sits one level in, with its own independent numbering, and the parent list's counter resumes correctly after the nested list closes. `</li>` now ends an item with a **single** line break (was falling through to `is_block_tag` → a `want=2` paragraph break, which spaced every item apart), so items read tight like a real browser. **Enabling fix in `wrap_text`:** leading spaces were dropped at column 0 (why tables use a leading `+`/`|`), which would have eaten the indent — it now **preserves leading spaces after a HARD `\n`** (a real line start) while still dropping them after a **soft word-wrap** (a `bol` flag), so an indent survives but wrapped continuation lines don't gain phantom spaces. **Pinned — `formtest` now 13/13:** a new case asserts `<ol>` → the exact lines `1. First` / `2. Second` / `3. Third`, and a `<ul>` with a nested `<ol>` → `- A` then the indented `  1. one` / `  2. two` then `- B` (parent marker back at column 0). `pngtest` 5/5 / `bmptest` 4/4 / `giftest` 7/7 / `jpegtest` 3/3 all still pass — the `<table>` layout KAT confirms the `wrap_text` change didn't regress bordered tables. **Verified live in the GUI, 0 faults** (screenshot): a numbered build-steps `<ol>` (1–4) with a nested `<ul>` under one step (`  - draw the desktop` / `  - draw the taskbar`) and a nested `<ol>` under another (`  1. open a tab` / `  2. load a page`), the parent numbering resuming at 3 and 4, plus a plain `<ul>` rendered as `-` bullets — every level indented and numbered correctly, items tight. **Honest scope:** numbering is always decimal starting at 1 (no `type=`/`start=` attributes, no a./i. styles); indent is a fixed 2 spaces/level and nesting is clamped at 8 deep. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files** (`gui/apps/selene_win.c`); `nyx-kernel.bin` 1659.1 -> 1660.4 KB. **Next:** `<pre>`/`<blockquote>` block formatting or definition lists, or begin selecting the last loose ends before the v5.9.100 general-maintenance pass — **~2 out** (v5.9.100 begins that pass: features stop, a maintenance sweep starts). |
| v5.9.97 | **Selene renders nested `<table>`s — a table inside a cell now flattens inline instead of corrupting the outer grid.** Closes the last table-renderer gap flagged at v5.9.77 ("a table inside a cell still renders only partially"). Root cause: `render_table`'s pass-1 cell-end scan (`gui/apps/selene_win.c`) **broke at a nested `<table>`**, so the inner table's `<tr>`/`<td>` were consumed as OUTER rows/cells — the inner grid leaked in, shifting every following column. Fix, three parts: (1) the **cell-end scan now skips a nested `<table>...</table>` whole** (depth-matched, brace-counting nested opens/closes) and keeps scanning for the cell's real `</td>`/`<tr>` boundary, so the inner rows never enter the outer occupancy grid; (2) a new **`sel_flatten_nested`** walks the nested table and emits a compact inline **`[ a b / c d ]`** (cells space-separated, rows separated by `" / "`) into the cell text, so the sub-table reads *in place* in the parent cell; (3) `sel_cell_text` calls it whenever it meets a nested `<table>` open. A table nested **two** levels deep collapses to a literal **`[..]`** (bounded — no unbounded recursion; the flatten's inner-cell scan breaks at any `<table>` so it never re-enters itself). The outer column is sized to the flattened string (capped at `SEL_TBL_COLCAP`), so alignment is preserved. **Pinned — `formtest` now 12/12:** a new case renders `<td><table><tr><td>A</td><td>B</td></tr><tr><td>C</td><td>D</td></tr></table></td>` inside a 2-column outer table and asserts the data line carries **`[ A B / C D ]`** *and* still has exactly **3 `|` borders** (i.e. 2 columns — the inner rows did not leak). `pngtest` 5/5 / `bmptest` 4/4 / `giftest` 7/7 / `jpegtest` 3/3 all still pass. **Verified live in the GUI, 0 faults** (screenshot): a Team/Scores/Rank table whose each "Scores" cell holds a 2×2 nested table renders as `| North | [ 10 12 / 8 15 ] | 1 |` / `| South | [ 7 9 / 11 6 ] | 2 |` — the Rank column (1, 2) stays aligned *after* the flattened cell, proving the outer grid is intact. **Honest scope:** a nested table is shown as a compact inline `[ ... ]` summary (its data kept in the parent cell), not as its own bordered sub-grid; deep (3rd-level) tables collapse to `[..]`. This suits a single-line-per-row ASCII renderer and keeps tabular data where it belongs. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files** (`gui/apps/selene_win.c`); `nyx-kernel.bin` 1657.2 -> 1659.1 KB. **Next:** the Selene table renderer is now feature-complete (layout + header + border + colspan/rowspan + nested); remaining polish could target `<pre>`/`<blockquote>` block formatting or nested lists, but the project is **~3 out** from the v5.9.100 general-maintenance pass (v5.9.100 begins that pass — features stop, a maintenance sweep starts). |
| v5.9.96 | **Selene tables handle `colspan` / `rowspan` — spanning headers and row labels now render with every column aligned.** Closes part of the v5.9.77 table gap ("no colspan/rowspan"). `render_table` (`gui/apps/selene_win.c`) gains an **occupancy grid**: pass 1 reads each cell's `colspan`/`rowspan` (new `sel_span_attr`) and **reserves its rectangular footprint** in the grid, so a later cell **skips past columns still covered by a rowspan above it** and lands in the correct column. A colspan cell's content is measured across its spanned columns and those columns are **grown until it fits**. The render loop then walks each row column-by-column: where a cell's **top-left origin** sits it draws the content **across its `cspan`** (combined column width, internal separators absorbed), advancing past the covered columns; every other grid position — empty, or continuing a colspan/rowspan — is drawn **blank**, so a rowspan label shows once with aligned blank continuation rows. `sel_tcell_t` gained `cspan`/`rspan`. **Pinned — `formtest` now 11/11:** a `<th colspan="2">Group</th>` renders as one wide **`| GROUP |`** cell over the `| A | B |` data row, and a `<td rowspan="2">` keeps the next row's cell aligned (**`|   | y |`** — column 0 blank under the span, `y` in column 1); the original `<table>` layout test still passes (the rewrite didn't regress plain tables). `pngtest` 5/5 / `bmptest` 4/4 / `giftest` 7/7 intact. **Verified live in the GUI, 0 faults** (screenshot): a Region/Sales table — **"Sales" spanning the Q1+Q2 columns** and **"North" spanning two rows** — renders with the header centred over its two columns and North's second data row (`8 | 9`) aligned under a blank column-0, i.e. every column lines up. **Honest scope:** the horizontal rules stay full-width (no border-splitting at a span boundary), and a rowspan is shown as its label in the first row + blank continuation rows (not a vertically-merged bordered cell). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files** (`gui/apps/selene_win.c`); `nyx-kernel.bin` 1655.4 -> 1657.2 KB. **Next:** nested `<table>`s (a table inside a cell still renders only partially), or other polish, as the project nears the v5.9.100 general-maintenance pass. (Reminder: v5.9.100 begins that pass — ~4 out.) |
| v5.9.95 | **Selene follows HTTP redirects (301/302/303/307/308) — a URL or link that redirects now just loads the destination.** Closes the gap noted at v5.9.68 ("Selene doesn't auto-follow 301s"). Two parts: (1) **`http_parse_response` now extracts the `Location:` header** — a new case-insensitive `http_get_header` helper + a `location[256]` field on `http_response_t`. (2) **`selene_load_ex` wraps its fetch in a redirect loop** (≤ **5 hops**): a 3xx response carrying a Location **re-fetches the resolved target** and repeats. The target is run through the existing `selene_resolve`, so **absolute *and* relative** Locations work (`https://host/x`, `/x`, or `x`), and the **URL bar updates to the final URL** at each hop. Method handling follows browsers: **301/302/303 downgrade to GET** (body dropped); **307/308 keep the method + body**. It also adds a **fetch retry** — NyxOS's first *new* TCP connection right after another fetch sometimes fails (v5.9.83), and a redirect chain opens several connections back-to-back, so without the retry the last hop could spuriously fail. **Pinned — `formtest` now 10/10:** a new case feeds a canned `HTTP/1.1 301 … Location: https://example.com/final` to `http_parse_response` and asserts `status_code == 301` and the extracted `location`. `pngtest` 5/5 / `bmptest` 4/4 / `giftest` 7/7 all still pass. **Verified live in the GUI, 0 faults** (screenshot): a **two-hop chain** served to the guest — `/` **302 →** `/hop1` **301 →** `/final` — is followed automatically; Selene renders the `/final` destination page ("REDIRECT FOLLOWED!") and the **URL bar reads `…/final`**, not the path first requested. **Honest scope:** capped at 5 hops (past that it renders the last 3xx response as-is); cross-scheme http↔https redirects work since each hop re-parses the scheme. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files** (`net/http.c`+`.h`, `gui/apps/selene_win.c`); `nyx-kernel.bin` 1654.4 -> 1655.4 KB. *(First feature increment after the kernel/ folder reorg — files now live under `net/`, `gui/apps/`, etc.)* **Next:** table `colspan`/`rowspan` or nested tables, or other polish, as the project nears the v5.9.100 general-maintenance pass. (Reminder: v5.9.100 begins that pass — ~5 out.) |
| v5.9.94 | **Selene decodes ~30 HTML entities (named + numeric) to readable text — closing the `&middot;`-renders-literally gap noted back at v5.9.78.** `decode_entity` used to know only `&amp; &lt; &gt; &quot; &apos; &nbsp;` and single ASCII code points; now it is table-driven and can emit a **short string**, so multi-character symbols come through readably. A new **`SEL_ENTITIES`** table maps each entity (by **name and Unicode code point**) to a display string: `&copy;`→"(c)", `&reg;`→"(r)", `&trade;`→"(tm)", `&mdash;`→"--", `&ndash;`→"-", `&hellip;`→"...", curly quotes `&ldquo;/&rdquo;/&lsquo;/&rsquo;`→ straight `"`/`'`, `&laquo;/&raquo;`→"<<"/">>", `&middot;/&bull;`→"*", `&times;`→"x", `&divide;`→"/", `&plusmn;`→"+/-", `&frac12;/14;/34;`→"1/2"/"1/4"/"3/4", `&euro;`→"EUR", `&pound;`→"GBP", `&sect;/&para;`, and `&deg;`→**°** (the CP437 degree glyph, 0xF8 — the font is a full 256-glyph VGA set). The **numeric** forms `&#nn;` / `&#xhh;` resolve through the *same* table by code point (so `&#8212;`→"--", `&#176;`→"°"), falling back to the raw byte for ASCII or "?" otherwise. Both entity call sites — `render_html` and the `<table>`-cell text extractor — now loop over the multi-character result (with the existing whitespace-collapsing + heading upper-casing preserved per output char). **Pinned — `formtest` now 9/9:** a new case renders `A&mdash;B&hellip;C&copy;D&amp;E&nbsp;F&middot;G&#8212;H` and confirms the decoded text **`A--B...C(c)D&E F*G--H`**; the eight existing cases (form / fields / GET / POST / `<img>` / `<table>` / tabs / image-loader) all still pass, proving the single-char→string refactor was clean. `pngtest` 5/5 / `bmptest` 4/4 / `giftest` 7/7 intact. **Verified live in the GUI, 0 faults** (screenshot): a page of ~25 entities renders every one correctly — em/en dashes, ellipsis, `(c)/(r)/(tm)`, `72°F` (the degree sign confirming the CP437 font), straight quotes from curly, `<<guillemets>>`, `1/2 1/4 3/4`, and the numeric forms. **Honest scope:** the single-byte font means most symbols are ASCII approximations (only `°` is a real glyph); an entity not in the table still renders literally. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files** (`selene_win.c` only); `nyx-kernel.bin` 1652.9 -> 1654.4 KB. **Next:** nested `<table>`s, or other small polish, as the project nears the v5.9.100 general-maintenance pass. (Reminder: v5.9.100 begins that pass — ~6 out.) |
| v5.9.93 | **GIF loop count honoured — animated GIFs now stop after their specified number of plays instead of looping forever.** v5.9.88 looped every animated GIF endlessly; now Selene respects the **NETSCAPE2.0 Application Extension** loop count. `gif_decode_anim` parses that extension (`21 FF 0B "NETSCAPE2.0" 03 01 LL HH`) into a new `gif_anim_t.loop_count` (**0 = infinite, N = play N times**; default 0 when a GIF omits the extension, so nothing that used to loop regresses — only an explicit finite count stops). Selene's `sel_img_t` gains `loop_count` + `loops_done`; `selene_anim_tick` counts each completed loop and, for a finite count, **freezes on the last frame** once it has played that many times (a finished animation stops ticking, like an off-screen one — no wasted CPU). **Pinned — `giftest` now 7/7:** a new loop-count case asserts a **loop=3** GIF reports `loop_count 3` and a **loop=0** GIF reports `0` (infinite); the six static/animated cases + `pngtest` 5/5 / `bmptest` 4/4 / `formtest` 8/8 all still pass. **Verified live in the GUI, 0 faults** (two screenshots + server log): a **loop=2** moving-disc `.gif` served to the guest **plays twice and then freezes on its last frame** (the disc parked at the far right, label "f6") — the two screenshots taken 1.6 s apart are **pixel-identical (0 changed pixels)**, versus the 1221-px diff v5.9.88's infinite loop produced, proving the animation actually stopped. **Honest scope:** the loop count is respected per-image; a GIF with no NETSCAPE extension still loops forever (the safe default). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files** (`gif.c` + `gif.h` + `gif_vectors.h` + `selene_win.c` extended); `nyx-kernel.bin` 1652.2 -> 1652.9 KB. **Next:** more small polish (more HTML entities in `decode_entity`, nested tables) or a new direction, as the project nears the v5.9.100 general-maintenance pass. (Reminder: v5.9.100 begins that pass — ~7 out.) |
| v5.9.92 | **RSA-PKCS1-SHA512 ServerKeyExchange verification (`0x0601`) — NyxOS now verifies EVERY common SKE signature scheme.** The last unverified SKE scheme lands, completing the set. New **`rsa_pkcs1_sha512_verify`** in `rsa.c`: the same EMSA-PKCS1-v1.5 padding as the SHA-256 path, but the **SHA-512 DigestInfo** prefix and a 64-byte hash — and the shared work (recover `EM = sig^e mod N`, then check `00 01 FF.. 00 ‖ DigestInfo ‖ hash`) is factored into two helpers (`rsa_recover_em` + `rsa_pkcs1_check`) that **both** the SHA-256 and SHA-512 verifiers now call. `tls.c`'s SKE dispatch gains a **`0x0601`** branch: it hashes **SHA-512(client_random ‖ server_random ‖ ServerECDHParams)** and verifies it against the leaf certificate's RSA key, folded into strict mode's `ske_ok` gate exactly like the other schemes (and `0x0601` was already advertised in our ClientHello's `signature_algorithms`). **Pinned — `rsatest` now 7/7:** two new cases — an **RSA-2048 PKCS1-v1.5 SHA-512** signature **accepted**, and a **tampered hash rejected** (vector from the `cryptography` library) — plus the **five existing cases (PKCS1-SHA256 ×3, PSS ×2) all still pass**, proving the helper refactor didn't disturb the proven paths. Regressions intact (`jpegtest` 3/3, `giftest` 6/6, `formtest` 8/8), **0 faults**. **Completeness milestone:** NyxOS's TLS now verifies the SKE signature for **every common scheme — ECDSA-P256/SHA-256, ECDSA-P384/SHA-384, RSA-PKCS1-SHA256, RSA-PSS-SHA256, and now RSA-PKCS1-SHA512** — so strict mode can enforce key-exchange authenticity no matter which one a server chooses. **Honest scope:** like ECDSA-P384 (`0x0503`, v5.9.79), RSA-SHA512 for SKE is rare on today's web, so the `tls.c` `0x0601` dispatch is **KAT-proven and structurally identical to the live-verified `0x0401`/`0x0804` RSA branch**, but not yet exercised by a live server (no reachable host was found that picks it). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files** (`rsa.c` + `rsa.h` + `tls.c` extended); `nyx-kernel.bin` 1649.5 -> 1652.2 KB. **Next:** honouring the GIF loop-count, or other small polish, as the project approaches the v5.9.100 general-maintenance pass. (Reminder: v5.9.100 begins that pass — ~8 out.) |
| v5.9.91 | **"Fancy" chroma upsampling for JPEG — 4:2:0 photos now decode as sharply as a reference decoder.** v5.9.89's decoder upsampled subsampled chroma with **nearest-neighbour** (each chroma sample replicated across its block), which is blocky at colour edges; this replaces it with libjpeg's **triangle-filter ("fancy") upsampling** for the two common photo layouts — **4:2:2 (h2v1)** and **4:2:0 (h2v2)** — where each output chroma sample is **¾ of the nearer source sample + ¼ of the farther** (h2v2 does that separably in both axes, a 9:3:3:1 weighting; image-edge rows/cols replicate). Each component is now pre-upsampled to a full-resolution plane — fancy where a dimension is exactly 2×, nearest for any other ratio (exactly libjpeg's rule) — then colour-converted. New `jpeg_fancy_h2v1` / `jpeg_fancy_h2v2` mirror `jdsample.c` byte-for-byte. **Measured by `jpegtest` with tightened tolerances (down from v5.9.89's 14/250):** the **4:2:0** case drops from **maxdiff 4 / meandiff 0.36** (nearest) to **maxdiff 2 / meandiff 0.01** (fancy) versus Pillow's libjpeg *fancy* decode — i.e. now essentially **IDCT-rounding-only**, proving the filter matches the reference; grayscale stays maxdiff 1 and 4:4:4 maxdiff 2 (no chroma to upsample). **`jpegtest` 3/3, and `pngtest` 5/5 / `bmptest` 4/4 / `giftest` 6/6 / `formtest` 8/8 all still pass, 0 faults.** The 4:2:0 KAT *is* a real decode through `jpeg_decode`, so it exercises the new upsampling path fully; Selene's JPEG rendering is unchanged from v5.9.90 (already proven live). **Honest scope:** fancy covers h2v1 + h2v2 (libjpeg's own fancy set); rarer ratios (h1v2, ≥3×) keep nearest, and the small extra memory is a full-res plane per subsampled component. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files** (`jpeg.c` extended); `nyx-kernel.bin` 1648.5 -> 1649.5 KB. **Next:** honouring the GIF loop-count, TLS (`0x0601`, the last unverified SKE scheme), or other polish. (Reminder: v5.9.100 begins a general maintenance pass — ~9 out.) |
| v5.9.90 | **JPEG wired into Selene — the browser shows REAL PHOTOS. The image arc is complete: PNG + BMP + GIF (static & animated) + JPEG.** The payoff of v5.9.89's decoder: one `else if` in Selene's magic-byte `<img>` dispatch — **`FF D8`→`jpeg_decode`** — since `jpeg_decode` yields the same `image_t` as png/bmp, a JPEG is just another static image on the existing fetch + scale + blit path (no animation, unlike GIF). A two-line change (`#include "jpeg.h"` + the dispatch branch) in `selene_win.c`. **Verified live in the GUI, 0 faults** (screenshot + server log): a **baseline 256×192 sunset `.jpg`** served to the guest is fetched by the per-frame tick, **decoded entirely in-kernel** (Huffman → dequantise → integer IDCT → YCbCr→RGB) and **drawn inline scaled into its box** — the sky gradient, the glowing sun disc and the horizon band all come out correct, matching the source photo. So **NyxOS's browser now renders real JPEG photographs**, not just synthetic PNGs. Regressions intact: the same page's other formats still render, and `jpegtest`/`giftest`/`pngtest`/`bmptest`/`formtest` are unchanged. **Honest scope:** inherits the decoder's limits (baseline JPEG only — progressive/arithmetic/CMYK rejected; nearest-neighbour chroma upsampling) and Selene's transport cap (a plain-HTTP image must fit the 64 KB response buffer — JPEG is compact so a real photo fits easily; HTTPS gets 512 KB). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1648.5 -> 1648.6 KB (just the dispatch branch). **Image arc DONE** (v5.9.81–90): inflate → PNG → render → lazy/progressive → BMP → GIF → animated GIF → JPEG decode → JPEG in Selene. **Next:** libjpeg-style *fancy* chroma upsampling (sharper 4:2:0), honouring the GIF loop-count, TLS (`0x0601`), or other polish. (Reminder: v5.9.100 begins a general maintenance pass — ~10 out.) |
| v5.9.89 | **A baseline JPEG decoder (`jpeg.c`) — NyxOS can now turn JPEG bytes into pixels, the last major web image format.** New **`kernel/jpeg.c` + `jpeg.h`** decode a **baseline (sequential DCT, Huffman-coded, 8-bit)** JPEG — the format of essentially every web photo. It walks the marker segments (**SOI / APPn / DQT / SOF0 / DHT / DRI / SOS / EOI**), builds the Huffman tables (canonical min/max/valptr), decodes each 8×8 block (**DC** diff-from-predictor + **run/size AC** coefficients over the entropy stream, with 0xFF00 de-stuffing and **restart markers**), **dequantises**, runs an **8×8 separable inverse DCT**, then **upsamples the chroma** and does **YCbCr→RGB** into a flat **RGBA** `image_t`. Supports **grayscale (1 component)** and **YCbCr (3 components) at 4:4:4 / 4:2:2 / 4:2:0** sampling. **Integer-only by necessity — the payoff of a real gotcha:** the kernel is built **`-mno-sse` (no float)**, so both the IDCT and the colour transform are **fixed-point** — the IDCT basis is stored ×2¹³ and accumulated in two `int64` passes (a `>>28` folds in both ½ normalisations + the level shift), and YCbCr→RGB uses the libjpeg integer constants (×2¹⁶). Writing it in `float` would have compiled to x87 / hit the x86-64 SSE-ABI and faulted or mis-run in kernel mode; the fixed-point form is the correct kernel-safe design. **Pinned by a new `jpegtest`, 3/3:** a **grayscale**, a **4:4:4** and a **4:2:0** test image are each decoded and compared to **Pillow's (libjpeg) reference decode within a tolerance** — byte-exactness is impossible across IDCT implementations — and the result is **essentially exact: maxdiff 1 / 2 / 4 and meandiff 0.01 / 0.02 / 0.36 per channel** (the tiny 4:2:0 gap is nearest-neighbour vs libjpeg's *fancy* chroma upsampling). Vectors were produced by Pillow (`scratchpad/gen_jpeg.py`). **`pngtest` 5/5, `bmptest` 4/4, `giftest` 6/6, `formtest` 8/8 all still pass, 0 faults.** **Honest scope:** **baseline only** — progressive (SOF2), arithmetic coding, 12-bit, and 4-component CMYK/YCCK are **rejected cleanly** rather than mis-decoded; chroma upsampling is nearest-neighbour (not libjpeg's fancy triangle filter); capped at 4096/side and 2 Mpx. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **NEW files `kernel/jpeg.c` + `kernel/jpeg.h` + `kernel/jpeg_vectors.h`** (git add) + `Makefile` `jpeg.o`; new command **`jpegtest`**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1627.6 -> 1648.5 KB. **Next — the payoff (v5.9.90):** wire `jpeg_decode` into Selene's `<img>` magic-byte dispatch (**`FFD8`→`jpeg_decode`**, one `else if`, since it yields the same `image_t` as png/bmp/gif), so the browser shows **real photos**, then a live web-photo test. (Reminder: v5.9.100 begins a general maintenance pass — ~11 out.) |
| v5.9.88 | **Animated GIF playback — Selene's GIFs now MOVE, driven by v5.9.87's per-frame tick.** v5.9.86 decoded only a GIF's first frame; now the whole animation plays. New **`gif_decode_anim`** (`gif.c`/`gif.h`) walks **every** Image Descriptor and composites each onto a logical-screen **canvas**, honouring the per-frame **sub-rectangle**, **transparent index**, and **disposal method** (leave / restore-to-background / restore-to-previous), producing an array of ready-to-blit RGBA **frames each with its delay**. A shared **`gif_read_frame`** (LZW-decode → de-interlaced, row-ordered indices) was factored out and is reused by both the static **`gif_decode`** (unchanged) and the animator; frame memory is capped (≤256 frames, ≤8 MB total — a huge GIF simply truncates). In Selene, `sel_img_t` gained the animation state (`frames`/`nframes`/`cur_frame`/`anim_ms`); the GIF branch of the magic-byte dispatch now calls `gif_decode_anim`, and a new **`selene_anim_tick`** advances each **on-screen** animated GIF by the ~33 ms compositor tick — flipping to the next frame (looping) when its delay elapses and repointing `px` at it — wired into `selene_win_tick` right beside the one-image-per-frame loader from v5.9.87. Off-screen GIFs freeze (no wasted CPU); a shared **`selene_img_free`** frees the frames array (with `px` only aliasing the current frame, never double-freed). **Pinned — `giftest` now 6/6:** two new animated cases — **anim-full** (3 full-screen frames, delays 10/15/20 cs) and **anim-partial** (3 sub-rectangle frames) — each decode to the **exact composited RGBA and per-frame delay**, **cross-checked frame-by-frame against Pillow's own decode** of the same bytes (so the sub-rect + transparency + disposal compositing is verified against a reference); the 4 static GIF cases + `pngtest` 5/5 + `bmptest` 4/4 + `formtest` 8/8 all still pass. **Verified live in the GUI, 0 faults** (two screenshots + server log): an **8-frame moving-disc `.gif`** served to the guest is fetched via the tick and **plays** — between two shots 0.6 s apart the purple disc advances across its box and the frame counter goes **1/8 → 7/8** (1221 px changed in the image region), proving `selene_win_tick` steps the frames. **Honest scope:** loops forever (the NETSCAPE loop-count is ignored); disposal 2 clears to transparent (the browser convention, not the deprecated background-colour); ≤8 MB of frames kept; still the cooperative single-thread model (a slow fetch briefly blocks — no worker thread, `kernel_thread_create` is a stub). **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files** (`gif.c`/`gif.h`/`gif_vectors.h` + `selene_win.c`/`.h` extended); `nyx-kernel.bin` 1621.7 -> 1627.6 KB. **Next:** JPEG (DCT — a big lift) for photos, honouring the GIF loop-count, a true background-thread fetch once real kernel threads exist, or back to TLS (`0x0601`) / other polish. (Reminder: v5.9.100 begins a general maintenance pass — ~12 out.) |
| v5.9.87 | **Cooperative, non-blocking image loading — the browser never freezes while pictures download.** v5.9.84 made image fetching lazy (only on-screen `<img>`s), but each page still **downloaded every visible picture in one synchronous burst before you could touch anything** — an image-heavy page locked the whole UI for seconds. Now Selene loads images **one per compositor frame.** The ~30fps **`on_tick`** callback (until now games-only) drives Selene too: each tick it fetches + decodes **at most one** visible, not-yet-tried image and asks for a redraw, so the page **appears instantly** and pictures **stream in one at a time** while **scrolling, clicks and tab-switches are handled in between.** The old blocking `selene_fetch_visible` loop is gone from both the load and scroll paths; a new **`selene_win_tick`** does the per-frame work through the pure, unit-tested **`selene_next_img_index`** (first visible + untried + un-decoded image, or none). The tick contract changed from `void` to **`int` (1 = "I changed something, redraw")**, so an idle browser **no longer forces a 30fps recomposite** — the three games keep their always-animate behaviour by returning 1. **Pinned — `formtest` now 8/8:** a new *image-loader visibility gating* case drives `selene_next_img_index` through scroll / tried / decoded transitions and asserts the exact pick sequence **(0, -1, 1, -1)**; `pngtest` 5/5, `bmptest` 4/4 and `giftest` 4/4 all still pass. **Verified live in the GUI, 0 faults** (screenshots + server log): a page with **three real PNGs** — the tick fetches **all three** (the server logs `GET /a.png`, `/b.png`, `/c.png` right after the page) and each is **decoded and blitted** (a purple disc, a teal square, an orange diagonal — real pixels, not `[img: alt]` boxes); and **Pong still animates** (a two-frame pixel diff confirms the ball + AI paddle moved), proving the `int`-return tick loop didn't disturb the games. **Honest scope:** this is **cooperative** single-threaded loading, not a worker thread — a *single* image's download still briefly blocks that one frame (true parallel fetch needs real kernel threads, and `kernel_thread_create` is still a stub) — but the multi-image freeze is gone and the UI stays live between images. **No new syscalls** (still 57); **no new files**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1621.2 -> 1621.7 KB (touches `selene_win.c/.h`, `compositor.c/.h`, and the three `*_win.c/.h` game ticks). **Next:** animated-GIF playback (reuse `on_tick` to advance frames), JPEG (DCT — a big lift), a true background-thread fetch once real kernel threads exist, or back to TLS (`0x0601`) / other polish. (Reminder: v5.9.100 begins a general maintenance pass — ~13 out.) |
| v5.9.86 | **GIF support — the third image format, with a real LZW decompressor. Selene now shows PNG, BMP and GIF.** New **`kernel/gif.c` + `gif.h`** decode the first image of a **GIF87a/GIF89a**: the header + Logical Screen Descriptor, a global or per-image local **color table**, the **transparent color index** (from a Graphic Control Extension), and the Image Descriptor's pixel data — **LZW-decompressed** (variable-width codes, clear/EOI, the dictionary with the KwKwK special case and the code-size bump), then **de-interlaced** (the 4-pass GIF row order) and mapped through the palette to RGBA. It slots straight into v5.9.85's magic-byte dispatch (`‰PNG`→png, `BM`→bmp, **`GIF`→gif**), so Selene picks the decoder from the bytes. **Pinned by a new `giftest`, 4/4:** a basic palette image, a **transparency** case (a color index → alpha 0), an **interlaced** image, and a **larger image that grows the LZW dictionary** (forcing the code-size to increase and the table to fill) each decode to the **exact expected RGBA**; the GIFs were encoded by Pillow (a correct GIF-LZW encoder) and every case **cross-checked against Pillow's own decode**. **`pngtest` still 5/5 and `bmptest` still 4/4** — no regression. **Verified live in the GUI, 0 faults** (screenshot): an **interlaced 180×130 `.gif`** served to the guest is fetched, LZW-decompressed, de-interlaced and **drawn inline scaled into its box** — the gradient + disc come out un-scrambled, proving the interlace pass end-to-end. **Honest scope:** the **first frame only** (GIF animation / later frames are ignored), and GIF's rare Plain-Text extension is skipped. **No new syscalls** (still 57); new files **`kernel/gif.c` + `gif.h` + `gif_vectors.h`** (git add); new command **`giftest`**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1615.2 -> 1621.2 KB. **Next:** JPEG (DCT — a big lift) for photos, a background-thread image fetch (true non-blocking), animated-GIF playback, or back to TLS (`0x0601`) / other polish. (Reminder: v5.9.100 begins a general maintenance pass — ~14 out.) |
| v5.9.85 | **BMP image support + a format-agnostic image pipeline — Selene now shows `.bmp` as well as `.png`.** A second image format, added cleanly. The decoded-image type is generalized from `png_image_t` to a shared **`image_t`** (new `image.h`) so every decoder produces the same thing, and Selene now **dispatches by the file's magic bytes** — `‰PNG` → `png_decode`, `BM` → the new `bmp_decode` — which drops in future formats with one `else if`. New **`kernel/bmp.c` + `bmp.h`** decode the uncompressed (**BI_RGB**) `BITMAPINFOHEADER` shapes still seen on the web: **24-bit BGR**, **32-bit BGRX**, and **8-bit palette**, stored **bottom-up or top-down** (negative height), with each scanline padded to a 4-byte boundary — expanded to RGBA (32-bit is treated opaque, per the BI_RGB spec, matching Pillow). **Pinned by a new `bmptest`, 4/4:** a 24-bit, a 32-bit, an 8-bit-palette, and a **top-down** BMP each decode to the **exact expected RGBA**; the vectors were produced by Python and **cross-checked against Pillow**. **`pngtest` still 5/5** — the `image_t` rename didn't disturb PNG. **Verified live in the GUI, 0 faults** (screenshot): a **24-bit `.bmp`** served to the guest is fetched, decoded and **drawn inline scaled into its box**, exactly like a PNG (same lazy fetch + blit path from v5.9.83/84). **Honest scope:** uncompressed BI_RGB only (no RLE / BITFIELDS / sub-8-bit); and because BMP is uncompressed it's **large**, so over plain HTTP the 64 KB response buffer caps a BMP at ~145×145 (bigger ones truncate and keep the placeholder — HTTPS gets a 512 KB buffer). **No new syscalls** (still 57); new files **`kernel/image.h` + `kernel/bmp.h` + `kernel/bmp.c`** (git add); new command **`bmptest`**; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1612.3 -> 1615.2 KB. **Next:** GIF (LZW, moderate — like inflate) or JPEG (DCT, a big lift), a background-thread image fetch, or back to TLS (`0x0601`) / other Selene polish. (Reminder: v5.9.100 begins a general maintenance pass — ~15 out.) |
| v5.9.84 | **Lazy + progressive image loading — an image-heavy page shows its text instantly and only downloads the pictures you can actually see.** v5.9.83 fetched *all* of a page's `<img>`s synchronously before showing anything, so an image-heavy page stalled on every picture. Now `selene_load_ex` **renders and paints the page first** (`compositor_redraw_now`, images as placeholders), then **`selene_fetch_visible`** downloads only the images whose box is currently on-screen — redrawing after each so they **pop in one at a time**. Scrolling calls **`selene_after_scroll`** (repaint immediately, then fetch any newly-visible images), so off-screen pictures are fetched **only when you scroll to them**. A per-image **`tried`** flag means a fetched or failed (e.g. 404) image is never re-requested. Refactored into `selene_fetch_one` (download+decode one image, with the v5.9.83 retry), `selene_img_anchor` (which line an image's box sits on), `selene_fetch_visible`, and `selene_after_scroll`. **Verified live in the GUI, 0 faults** (two screenshots + the server log): a **taller-than-a-screen** page with three images — on load, the **text appears immediately** and **only the one visible image is downloaded** (the server logs exactly one `GET /nyx.png` per page load, images two and three untouched); after **scrolling down**, images two and three are fetched **as they enter the viewport** (two further `GET /nyx.png`) and render in place. **Honest scope:** each individual fetch is still synchronous (it briefly blocks the UI while that one image downloads — true background fetching would need a worker thread), but the page no longer waits on off-screen images, which was the actual stall. **No new syscalls** (still 57); no new files; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1611.5 -> 1612.3 KB (all in `selene_win.c`). **Next:** more image formats (BMP/GIF, or the bigger JPEG), a background-thread fetch for true non-blocking loads, or back to TLS (`0x0601`) / other Selene polish. (Reminder: v5.9.100 begins a general maintenance pass — ~16 out.) |
| v5.9.83 | **Selene draws real images — the payoff of the image arc: `<img>` PNGs are now fetched, decoded and rendered inline.** v5.9.81 (inflate) + v5.9.82 (PNG decode) built the pipeline; this wires it into the browser. After a page renders, **`selene_fetch_images`** downloads the first few `<img>` sources (HTTP or TLS, same machinery as the page fetch), and `png_decode`s each into RGBA stored on the `sel_img_t`. Images become **block-level boxes** (`SEL_IMG_BOX_W`×`SEL_IMG_BOX_LINES` = 192×90 px) that reserve their own vertical space; a decoded image is **aspect-fit scaled** (nearest-neighbour) and **alpha-composited** over the page, while anything that fails to fetch/decode (or isn't PNG) keeps the framed **`[img: alt]`** placeholder. Decoded pixels are freed on navigation and tab-close; the fetch is bounded (≤ 8 images/page, ≤ 512 KB each). **A real bug the live test caught + fixed:** the *first* new TCP connection right after the page fetch sometimes fails, so with two identical `<img src>` only the second rendered — the fetch now **retries once**, and both come through. **Verified live in the GUI, 0 faults** (screenshot): a page served to the guest with a 220×150 PNG (a purple gradient + white crescent moon + stars) renders **both copies as the actual image, scaled into their boxes**, with ordinary text flowing above and below, and a deliberately-missing `/missing.png` correctly showing **`[img: missing image]`** — the server log confirms `GET /` then two `GET /nyx.png` (200) + `GET /missing.png` (404). **So NyxOS's browser now shows real pictures.** **Honest scope:** PNG only (per `png.c`: 8-bit, non-interlaced), the image fetch is synchronous (it blocks the page load briefly), and the display box is a fixed size. **No new syscalls** (still 57); no new files; `nyx-kernel.bin` 1609.8 -> 1611.5 KB (all in `selene_win.c`). **Next:** more image polish (JPEG/GIF/BMP, or lazy/async fetch so image-heavy pages don't stall the load), or back to TLS (`0x0601`) / other Selene work. (Reminder: v5.9.100 begins a general maintenance pass — ~17 out.) |
| v5.9.82 | **A PNG decoder (`png.c`) on top of v5.9.81's inflate — NyxOS can now turn PNG bytes into pixels.** Step two of the real-image arc.
